So the shop showed a local change the server never received: an owner could narrow a role on screen while the tenant server went on deciding customer requests on the stored level (ADR-35; doc 08 DA-124 (2)). The owner brief for M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2 asks for that browser-to-server path to be made real for the built-in roles' Customers level, without describing the rest of the role matrix as server-enforced.

**Decision.**
1. **The session.** The shop app signs in to the tenant boundary through the M5 tenant-session contract (ADR-31): `POST /api/v1/session/login` with the signed-in user's Firebase ID token as `Authorization: Bearer` and `X-TMPOS-CSRF: 1`; the server verifies the token and mints the session (ADR-33), and `GET /api/v1/session` confirms it (`src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.ts`).
   - The session is the browser-managed HttpOnly `__Host-tmpos_tenant_session` cookie. The page holds only the session CSRF token, in memory, beside the UID it was issued for, and sends it as `X-TMPOS-Session-CSRF` on the PUT and on `POST /api/v1/session/logout`. The browser never receives or builds an issuer key, a mint proof or a database session key.
   - A session is reused only for the UID that established it. On a Firebase user switch the previous user's session is logged out before the next user's session is read or established (`ensure()` awaits any in-flight logout before it reads or establishes the next user's session), and every signed-out auth state, the first callback of a page load included, attempts a logout (`src/context/AccessContext.tsx`).
2. **The read-back route.** `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` (`server/runtime/roleLevelDirectory.ts`) sits on the edit's path, under the edit's owner rule `edit_role_levels`, with `trustedContext: 'required'`, `recentAuth: 'none'`, no body and no idempotency. Its tenant is the authorizer-selected trusted scope from the session's admission, passed as a parameter to 020's definer read `tmpos_role.m7a_role_levels(uuid, text)`, the three roles in one statement; unlike the customer read, it is not bound to the session key in the database (doc 08 DA-121). It answers the four built-in roles in the fixed order `store_owner`, `manager`, `technician`, `sales_staff`, each with `level`, `defaultLevel`, `source` (`fixed`; `stored`; `default` for no row or a NULL lock row) and `editable` (true for the three non-owner roles, whatever the source). The runtime handler (`roleLevelDirectoryOf`) validates the port's answer, and anything out of shape is a bounded 503. It writes no audit record and is `Cache-Control: no-store`. `TENANT_SHIPPING_ROUTES` adds it (session, no body, no idempotency): nine routes, beside ADR-34 decision 8's eight as ADR-35 amended it. Doc 05 §2 holds the contract.
3. **The panel.** The Employees permissions screen gains "Server-enforced Customers access" (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerLevels.tsx`), the only part of the screen that reaches the server: `store_owner` as "Full — fixed", and a level, a reason and "Save to server" for `manager`, `technician` and `sales_staff`. It reads on load, on a focus or visibility refresh (one at a time, skipped while a save or a sign-in confirmation is under way) and once per save's read-back. Grid changes to custom roles and other domains make no server call; the grid carries the banner "Changes in this table apply to this browser tab only. They are not saved to the server and are not enforced by it."
4. **"Saved" means server-confirmed.** A row says "Saved" only after an exact PUT 200 (a fresh execution or an idempotent replay of the same key) followed by a same-tenant read-back equal to the requested level. The key rules:
   - one `Idempotency-Key` per payload (role, level and reason). The list below is P3-R2's first version; later stages changed it (a 5xx or an unnamed body is now held, not settled, and among 409s only `request_in_progress` keeps the key), and the current rules are in [05](./05-canonical-data-ownership-and-api-db-contracts.md);
   - no response: one automatic retry under the same key (superseded by M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1-R1, ADR-46 decision 8: never re-sent automatically; held until the owner presses Save again);
   - 409: the key is held and the level read back; when it already equals the draft, "The server now has X, but this save is not confirmed yet. Press Save again to confirm.", and that retry replays the key;
   - a 5xx or a body out of contract: the key is settled and the level read back: "Not saved by this attempt. The server has X." (with " — it may have been set elsewhere" when X is the requested level, and "Not saved by this attempt, and the level could not be read back. Try again." when the read-back fails);
   - a 401, a step-up 403, any other 403, a 400 and a 422 each have their own text; the 403 is "Not saved: the server refused this change. Only the store owner can change these levels."

   A later read that moves a Saved level replaces it with "Changed elsewhere — the server now has X." Every asynchronous result applies only under the user and tenant that started it; a read-back naming another tenant restarts the panel and keeps "Your last change may have been applied to a different store. Review the levels below." until the next user change.
5. **Authority is unchanged.** The owner rule stays as R1 made it, and owner-only editing stays **flagged for owner confirmation**, contested and unresolved (DA-124). The brief lets a manager edit only with a durable, server-verifiable `manage_role_permissions` grant, and none exists. So a manager's read is refused 403 and the panel shows no levels, with the note "Server-enforced Customers access can only be viewed and changed by the store owner. Changes in the table below affect this browser only."; the PUT refuses a manager at the authorizer (403), whatever the body.

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *Manager editing without a durable grant.* A role name or a client-side flag is not authority, and doc 04's server-side parity and cap rules are undefined (DA-124).
- (b) *A tenant or version precondition on the PUT.* R1's contract is unchanged in this stage; a precondition is a later contract decision (DA-125). Until then the last writer wins, and each tab shows its own read-back.
- (c) *One global Save for the whole matrix.* Sending custom roles or other domains with the Customers PUT, or calling them saved, would imply enforcement the server does not do.
- (d) *A client-built session.* An issuer key, a proof or a database key in the browser, or a session made from client claims, is refused.
- (e) *A sign-out control in the shop.* Not added; a logout follows a Firebase sign-out or a user switch only (DA-127).

**Consequences.**
- **Nothing closes.** G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION stay OPEN, and DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
  - The existing customer list and create, `GET` and `POST /api/v1/customers`, decide on the stored level (ADR-35); the shop app does not call them, and `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}` did not exist yet at R2 *(it exists in code since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2, ADR-38; the shop app does not call it)* *(Amended M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5, ADR-39: the shop app's Customer directory page now calls all three customer routes; see the amendment below)*.
  - The tenant entry is not deployed, and the shop build and `/api/v1/*` must be served on one origin, the tenant host (DA-107).
- **Supersedes.** ADR-35's and doc 09's R1 statements that the shop editor is unchanged and calls no route are superseded for built-in Customers levels (ADR-35 amendment above).
- **Register.** DA-85 is narrowed again, not closed. DA-43, DA-107, DA-121 and DA-124 are amended. DA-125 to DA-127 are new:
  - R1's PUT has no tenant or version precondition (DA-125);
  - shop users need a PostgreSQL identity and one tenant grant, and no production provisioning exists (DA-126);
  - the shop has no sign-out control (DA-127).
- **Evidence.** Disposable PostgreSQL and local sockets only:
  - the unit suites at the network boundary: `src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.test.ts`, `src/tenant/roleLevelsClient.test.ts`, `src/tenant/ServerCustomerLevels.test.tsx`, `src/context/AccessContext.tenantSession.test.tsx` and `server/runtime/roleLevelDirectory.test.ts`;
  - `tests/db/roleLevels.integration.test.mjs` (M7RL-G01 to M7RL-G04 and M7RLH-C1, the compiled entry's nine routes) over the tenant entry;
  - `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs`: the production shop build in a real browser on `https://shop.tmpos.test` through CDP interception, with a synthetic identity provider faked at the browser boundary plus the Firestore emulator, and the real tenant entry over disposable PostgreSQL. In CI it is the step "Shop role levels in a real browser against the tenant entry and disposable PostgreSQL (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2)", in the runner's Chrome; local runs use Chromium.

  The customer and role-level route contracts are preserved; evidence counts belong in the stage report. 001–020 and the four frozen operator paths are byte-identical, with no new migration. Code-only: nothing is deployed, and no managed database was touched.

**Amended (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 1, ADR-37).** Alternative (b) deferred a tenant or version precondition and kept last-writer-wins. That deferral is superseded and last-writer-wins is rejected (ADR-37 alternative (c)). ADR-37 adopts the tenant-and-version precondition, and DA-125 is closed for the built-in Customers server-backed editor. Decision 2's read now goes through 021's `m7a_role_level_states(uuid)` and adds `version` to each role (still a bare trusted tenant id, DA-121). ADR-37 decision 5 replaces Decision 4's save rules. A new key per new attempt; an unresolved attempt held as its key plus its exact change until a 200 or a `write_conflict` settles it; a stale draft made an explicit conflict; a change shown as saved only on an exact 200 at the committed version and a same-tenant read-back at that version. Decision 4's final paragraph also changes. A read naming another tenant still restarts the panel with a persistent notice, but the notice now says no change was sent to the newly connected store. A user switch now mounts a fresh panel per user. Decisions 1, 3 and 5 stand.

**Amended (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5, ADR-39).** The first Consequence's "the shop app does not call them", and its note that the shop app does not call `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}`, are superseded: a separate page, the Customer directory, calls the list, the create and the detail read over the session of decision 1. Decision 1's "sends it as `X-TMPOS-Session-CSRF` on the PUT and on `POST /api/v1/session/logout`" now also covers the customer create, and the directory page is a second in-memory holder of the session CSRF token, under the same rule (no state, storage, URL, DOM or log). The session client's `rate-limited` answer now carries the server's `Retry-After` when a valid one was sent. The role panel and its save rules are unchanged.

**Amended (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1, ADR-40).** Decision 1's "a session is reused only for the UID that established it" now reads: a session is reused only for the user the server names as its owner. Every `ensure` reads `GET /api/v1/session` first and reuses the session, with no login, when the answer's `subject` equals the digest of the signed-in user's UID. So a page load, a reopen or a second tab no longer signs in again, and a session another identity left in the browser still leads to a login. The logout on a Firebase sign-out or user switch is unchanged. Decision 1's "sends it as `X-TMPOS-Session-CSRF` on the PUT and on `POST /api/v1/session/logout`", already extended to the customer create, now also covers `GET /api/v1/customers` (its cursor pages included) and `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}`; `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` still carries no token (doc 08 DA-147). The role panel shares this behaviour with the Customer directory, and its save rules are unchanged.

**Amended (M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1, ADR-46).** Decision 3's "the only part of the screen that reaches the server" and alternative (c) now cover two panels: beside "Server-enforced Customers access" the screen shows "Server-saved Invoices level" (`ServerInvoiceLevels`, the same component with a domain), which reads `GET /api/v1/roles/levels/invoices` and saves through the same PUT with domain `invoices`, under the same save rules. Each panel sends only its own domain; nothing sends the matrix as a whole, and the Invoices panel's copy says the level is saved on the server while invoices and their actions are not enforced by it. The two panels share decision 1's one session; a renewal in one lets the other re-read with no login (doc 08 DA-185). Custom roles, the remaining domains and deletion stay client-only, and owner-only editing stays flagged (DA-124).

## ADR-37 — An owner's role-level save asserts the tenant and version it reviewed, and a refusal commits nothing in its command transaction (DECIDED — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 1)

**Context.** ADR-36 connected the shop's role screen to R1's PUT, which wrote whatever it was sent to whichever tenant the presenting session named. The entry witnesses on `86c4ce8` (`tests/db/roleLevelPrecondition.integration.test.mjs`) showed two harms (doc 08 DA-125). An owner's draft for tenant A, sent after the same account moved to tenant B and signed in again, answered 200 and changed B, with an edit audit and a completion (P4W-01). A delayed older tab restored Create over a newer None, and a draft read before an edit and a reset to the default overwrote the reset (P4W-03, P4W-04). A save already in flight when the switch happened wrote nothing in B (P4W-02). The owner brief for M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 requires both assertions to be enforced in the committing database transaction, a refused save to write nothing, and client cancellation not to be relied on.

**Decision.**
1. **A pure per-row counter.** Migration 021 adds `tmpos_role_edit.role_level_version`, one row per (tenant, editable role, domain) whose level row has been updated since 021. No row means version 0. Nothing is seeded at apply, and no wall clock is used. The version is an inert integer bounded by CHECKs, with no foreign key.
2. **A trigger invariant.** An `AFTER UPDATE FOR EACH ROW` trigger on 020's `tmpos_role.tenant_role_level`, enabled `ALWAYS`, adds exactly one to the row's version on every UPDATE, by any path: 021's edit, 020's edit run by the table owner, a raw owner UPDATE, one that changes only `updated_at`, and a replica-mode session. Restoring a catalog default is an UPDATE, so no A → B → A cycle recreates a version a stale draft holds. This also covers a 020 edit that passed its EXECUTE check before 021 committed and then waited on 021's table lock: it completes after the apply and advances the version (`tests/db/roleLevelVersionRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M7RV-14). 021's edit reads the version back after its update and raises unless it is exactly the checked version plus one.
3. **A precondition, never a selector.** `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` carries `expectedTenant` and `expectedVersion`, from the read the owner reviewed. 021's `m7a_role_level_set_v2` still derives the owner, the grant and the tenant from the session key alone. It then checks the tenant before any insert, the version unlocked, and the version again on the version row FOR UPDATE after the role row FOR UPDATE and the clock recheck. Neither value names what is written or grants anything. `tmpos_app` loses EXECUTE on 020's unconditional edit, and gains USAGE on `tmpos_role_edit` and EXECUTE on the edit and on `m7a_role_level_states`, the one-snapshot read of levels and versions that `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` now makes.
4. **An unsealed 409 on refusal.** A failed precondition rolls the whole command transaction back: no level, version, audit record, event or idempotency completion. The runtime answers 409 `write_conflict` in its own `{ error, requestId }` body. Unlike the platform commands' version conflict (sealed since M5-GIAM-D1-P2-R1), it is not sealed, because the brief forbids a completion for a refused precondition. The key's lease is left to expire under the existing policy, and the same key and body are then decided afresh (`tests/db/roleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M7RLH-10). The sealed 200 names the committed version, `expectedVersion + 1`, so a replay after a later edit is still distinguishable from the current state.
5. **The panel.** Each draft carries the tenant and version it was made against, and each new attempt gets a new key.
   - **Conflicts.** A stale draft becomes a conflict showing the server's level and the unsaved choice, with Discard and Reapply. Reapply works only against the level read for that conflict, and is refused on a row shown at its last known level (one no successful read has established since).
   - **Held attempts.** An attempt whose outcome is unknown is held explicitly as its key plus the exact change it sent. A confirmation re-sends exactly that pair, and only a 200 or a `write_conflict` settles it. While it is held, a session failure or a refused re-send never says "Not saved". A changed reason or level is an explicit local abandonment of the held attempt, not a settlement: the panel stops tracking it, and its server outcome may stay unknown. After a changed reason the row becomes a conflict if the version moved, otherwise an ordinary draft; a changed level starts a new draft against the last read.
   - **Saved.** A change is shown as saved only on an exact 200 at `baseVersion + 1` and a same-tenant read-back of the requested level at that version. A later version reads as acknowledged, then changed elsewhere.
   - **Deferred reads.** A read deferred while a save was in flight is followed by a fresh read when that save ends without its own read-back.
   - **Tenant and user changes.** After a tenant change, the notice says no change was sent to the newly connected tenant. An answer to a save sent before it is only a detached notice. Each user gets their own panel instance, so a user switch drops everything, notices included.
   - **Scope.** Custom roles and other domains stay local-only.

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *A wall-clock or seeded version* (an earlier design: seed every existing row with the apply time, and take the greater of old + 1 and the clock on each edit). A clock stepped back further than a rollback-to-reapply interval could re-issue a version, and seeding adds an apply-time write for no guarantee the counter plus a fail-closed rollback do not already give. Rejected for a pure counter whose rollback refuses while any version exists (DA-128).
- (b) *Client cancellation only.* A request already sent cannot be recalled, and a cancelled `fetch` does not stop a server transaction; the brief states cancellation alone is insufficient. The browser cancels unsent saves and pending reads, but safety rests on the server precondition.
- (c) *Last-writer-wins* (R1's behaviour, ADR-36 alternative (b)). It let an older permissive draft silently restore Create over a newer restrictive level, and a draft for one tenant land on another (P4W-01, P4W-03, P4W-04).
- (d) *Sealing the precondition 409 as the completion*, as the platform commands' conflict is. It would make the key terminal and write a completion for a request that changed nothing, which the brief forbids.
- (e) *Adding the counter as a column or routine in 020's schema.* 020's postcondition pins its schema's inventory, and 020 must stay byte-identical, so 021 uses its own schema.

**Consequences.**
- **Closes.** DA-125, for the built-in Customers server-backed editor (doc 08; evidence and commit `e4ba510` there).
- **Nothing else closes.** G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION stay OPEN. DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions: a runtime-role holder with a live owner key can still edit directly through 021's routine, given the tenant and version the states read discloses (DA-119, DA-121). Owner-only editing stays flagged (DA-124).
- **Deploy and rollback.** The startup preflight requires exactly 001–021, and D4 names the chain through 021. 021 is forward-only in effect: its down refuses while any version row exists and does not re-grant 020's edit, so the pre-021 runtime cannot start until an operator re-grants it, which reopens DA-125. Roll 021 back before 020 (DA-122).
- **Register.** DA-125 closed as above. DA-119, DA-120, DA-121 and DA-122 amended. New: DA-128 (the counter restarts after a deliberate rollback), DA-129 (the table owner's unversioned writes) and DA-130 (020's apply-time postcondition no longer holds).
- **Evidence.** Disposable PostgreSQL and local sockets only:
  - `tests/db/roleLevelVersionRoutine.integration.test.mjs` (M7RV-01 to M7RV-16): apply and postcondition, inventory and effective grants, the read, refusals, tenant mismatch, version conflict, the applied edit, reset-to-default ABA, the create's lock row, two edits from one read, edit/create ordering, the down and re-apply, expiry during the wait, a 020 edit parked across the apply, every other update path, and the key move (an update that moves a row to another key advances both keys);
  - `tests/db/roleLevelPrecondition.integration.test.mjs` (P4W-01 to P4W-04), red on `86c4ce8` except P4W-02 and green on the final bytes, over two tenant-entry instances sharing one database;
  - `tests/db/roleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M7RLH-10 and the re-pinned M7RLH-08;
  - `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SRL-W, SRL-01 to SRL-12 and SRL-08T, of which SRL-08, SRL-08T and SRL-10 to SRL-12 are the P4 cases;
  - the unit suites of the route, the directory, the command transaction, the store, the mutator and the preflight, and of the shop panel (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerLevels.test.tsx`, SCL-01 to SCL-46, SCL-26 to SCL-46 new) and client (`src/tenant/roleLevelsClient.test.ts`, RLC-01 to RLC-09). The frontend runner's floor is 436 cases (`MIN_FRONTEND_CASES`).

  Two new CI steps run the database suites against the disposable service. Counts and exits: local gate on the `e4ba510` bytes: disposable-database gate 26 suites 525/525, shared-service CI replay 26 steps 479/479, shop browser gate 14/14 under the Firestore emulator, frontend 442/442, run-tests 159/159, 0 sentinel dials; `build:check` and the admin browser build were killed locally (SIGKILL) and are not counted as passed; exact-commit CI: run 36616730497, green on attempt 1. 001–020 and the four frozen operator paths (`scripts/supabase-migrate.ts`, `scripts/managed-baseline-launcher.mjs`, `scripts/managed-m005-launcher.mjs`, `server/platform-identity/migrationExecutor.ts`) are byte-identical to the entry commit. Code-only: nothing is deployed, and no managed database was touched.

**Amended (M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1, ADR-46).** The precondition, the trigger invariant and the unsealed 409 now also govern the built-in roles' Invoices level: 026's `tmpos_role_invoice.m7a_invoice_role_level_set` is decision 3's edit with the domain fixed to `invoices`, and 021's domain-agnostic trigger versions its rows per (tenant, role, domain), so an Invoices edit and a Customers edit of the same role never share a version. Decision 5's "Custom roles and other domains stay local-only" now reads: custom roles and every domain but Customers and Invoices stay local-only, and the Invoices level is configuration only (doc 08 DA-183). Decision 5's panel rules apply to both panels.

## ADR-38 — One customer by id: a single route template, authentication before the id, identical foreign and missing 404s, and a sealed Location (DECIDED — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2)

**Context.** The tenant plane could list and create customers (ADR-32 to ADR-35) but not read one. The owner brief for M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 asks for `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}`:
- under the list's permission, `view_customers`, and the current server-backed Customers level;
- with the tenant taken from the selected session scope only;
- with a canonical UUID id, and a bounded 400 for a malformed id if that matches existing conventions;
- with indistinguishable 404s for a missing id and another tenant's id;
- answering `{ id, name }` only;
- rechecked at the database read boundary as the list is;
- with no direct table SELECT for the login.

It also asks for `Location` on the create's 201, but only if the sealed idempotency response can replay it consistently. Until this stage the route table held literal paths only, matched exactly (`server/runtime/routes.ts`).

**Decision.**
1. **One template, admitted by name.** `routes.ts` admits exactly one templated route, `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}` (`TEMPLATED_ROUTES`); any other method, path or template is refused at registration (`route_path_invalid`).
   - The template is never idempotent. A literal path directly below its prefix is refused at registration, so no literal route can sit beside the template's segment.
   - A request matches it only as the prefix `/api/v1/customers`, one `/`, and ONE raw, undecoded, non-empty segment of 1–128 characters with no `/` (`MAX_PATH_ID_SEGMENT_LENGTH`).
   - A 129-character or longer segment, an empty segment, a trailing slash or an extra segment matches no route: the terminal bounded 404, as before.
   - Literal routes are still matched first and exactly. The authorizer sees the template path (`route.path`), never the id. The request log classifies the path as `other` (`classifyRoute`), so it carries no id.
2. **Authentication before the id.** The chain runs as for any tenant session read: the Host check, the limiter, the session, and the tenant authorizer deciding `view_customers` on the role's current level. An unauthenticated or unauthorized request gets the chain's 401 or 403 whatever the segment, so the id's shape is judged only after authorization. The handler (`createCustomerDetailRoute`, `server/runtime/customerDirectory.ts`) then refuses in this order:
   - any query, an empty one included: 400 `invalid_request`;
   - a missing trusted tenant scope or session key: 403;
   - a segment that is not a canonical lowercase UUID (`pathIdOf`; uppercase, %-encoded, braces, anything else): 400 `invalid_request`.
3. **The route-surface change (C2.5).** Every `GET /api/v1/customers/<segment of 1–128 characters>` is now an authenticated route: 401 or 403 before authentication and authorization, and 400 for a non-UUID segment after them. Before this stage it was the terminal 404. Template lookup is keyed by method, so any other method on such a path is still the terminal 404 (doc 08 DA-136).
4. **One port call per canonical id, one database routine.** A canonical id makes exactly one call, with no cache and no early answer, through `createPostgresCustomerDetail` (`server/persistence/postgresCustomerDirectory.ts`) to migration 022's `tmpos_customer_detail.m7a_customer_get(p_session_key, p_customer_id, p_expected_tenant)`. It is `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER` and session-bound, and on one snapshot it:
   - re-derives the tenant, grant and role from the session as 020's `m7a_customer_page_v3` does, and requires the role's current Customers level at View or above;
   - requires the derived tenant to equal the trusted scope's (a precondition, never a selector) before any customer row is visited;
   - re-runs 017's rechecks;
   - probes 017's primary key for the id, filtered to the derived tenant.

   `tmpos_app` gets USAGE on the schema and EXECUTE on the routine only, and still holds no privilege on the customer table (doc 05 §2).
5. **Indistinguishable foreign and missing 404s.** Another tenant's id and a missing id each make one routine call, take the same SQL statement and outcome branch, and answer the same row. They become the same status (404), the same error code and body shape (`{ error: 'not_found', requestId }`) and one bounded log reason (`customer_not_found`). Per-request metadata differs: the `requestId`, `X-Request-Id`, `Date` and similar headers. Their timing is not equalized, and the residual is unbounded by design. M7CD-05 asserts only a sanity bound on medians, max(2 ms, 25%), and logs them (doc 08 DA-135).
6. **The answer.** `200 { customer: { id, name } }`, nothing else: the create's 201 body shape and the list entry's two fields. It writes no audit record, like the list (doc 08 DA-131). The compiled tenant entry serves it (ten routes); the administrative entry does not.
7. **Location through the sealed envelope.** The create's plan now returns `headers: { location: '/api/v1/customers/<id>' }` (`server/runtime/customerCreate.ts`). The idempotency envelope's header allowlist is `location` (`server/runtime/idempotency.ts`), and the whole envelope is sealed into the completion, so a same-key replay on either instance answers the same body and the same `Location` with `Idempotent-Replayed: true`. A completion sealed before this build carried `headers: {}` and replays without `Location`, exactly as committed (doc 08 DA-134).

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *A generic router template or parameter syntax* (`/v1/{param}` anywhere). It would open every route family to parameterized paths and their prefix, case and encoding variants; the table admits the one template by name instead (C2.5).
- (b) *A terminal 404 for a malformed id* (the design's first draft, D5). The brief asks for a bounded 400 where conventions match, and the list's convention is a post-authentication 400 `invalid_request` for invalid input. The price is decision 3's route-surface change, recorded as DA-136.
- (c) *A tenant-first index to equalize foreign and missing timing.* It would change 017's table, and a tenant-filtered lookup still differs by what the heap visit finds; the residual is recorded (DA-135), not claimed away.
- (d) *Judging the id before authentication.* It would give an unauthenticated caller an id-shape oracle (400 vs 404 vs 401) on a tenant route; the chain decides first.
- (e) *A second, new runtime module.* The detail route, port and adapter live beside the list's, so the tenant artifact's module inventory is unchanged.

**Consequences.**
- **Nothing closes.** G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION stay OPEN, and DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
- **Deploy and rollback.** The startup preflight requires exactly 001–022 and 022's two grants, so 022 and the runtime move together. 022's rollback holds no data and removes only its schema; the order is 022, then 021, then 020 (doc 08 DA-133).
- **Register.** DA-131 to DA-136 are new.
- **Evidence.** Disposable PostgreSQL and local sockets only:
  - `tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs` (M7CD-01 to M7CD-09 and M7CD-02b): apply and postcondition, grants and indirect-path refusals, own/foreign/missing/invalid/unknown-key/foreign-tenant/revoked answers, list parity, timing sanity, the adapter and probe, no writes, rollback, and the out-of-order 020 case;
  - `tests/db/tenantEntry.integration.test.mjs` M7TE-14 and M7TE-C1 over the source and compiled tenant entry;
  - `tests/db/productionEntry.integration.test.mjs`, which pins that the administrative entry serves no detail path;
  - the unit suites of the route table, the handler, the adapter and the create's `Location`, including a pre-change envelope replayed without `Location`.

  A new CI step runs `customerDetail.integration.test.mjs`. Counts and exits: local gate on the `5a26e08` bytes: disposable-database gate 27 suites 536/536, shared-service CI replay 27 steps 490/490, shop browser gate 14/14 under the Firestore emulator, frontend and run-tests 159/159 green, 0 sentinel dials; `build:check` and the admin browser build were killed locally (SIGKILL) and are not counted as passed; commit `5a26e08`; exact-commit CI: run 36638041302, green on attempt 1. Code-only: nothing is deployed, and no managed database was touched.

**Amended (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6, ADR-41).** Decision 1's "exactly one templated route" is superseded: `routes.ts` admits exactly two templated routes by name, `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}` and `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile` (`TEMPLATED_ROUTES`), and still refuses every other template at registration. The second is the same id segment followed by exactly the literal `/profile`. Lookup is an exact literal first, then the template ending in `{id}`, then the template with `{id}` before its suffix; neither template is idempotent, and no literal route may sit anywhere below a template's prefix. Decision 3's route-surface change extends to `GET /api/v1/customers/<segment>/profile`; any other extra segment is still the terminal 404 (doc 08 DA-136, amended).

**Amended (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, ADR-42).** `routes.ts` admits exactly three templated routes by name; the third is `PATCH /api/v1/customers/{id}`, on the detail template's segment. Decision 1's "the template is never idempotent" is narrowed: a templated GET still takes no idempotency, and the closed set of templated commands (`IDEMPOTENT_TEMPLATED_ROUTES`, exactly that PATCH) must require idempotency and declare a `command`; every other combination is refused at registration. The runtime judges the PATCH's segment after the chain, binds the canonical id into the idempotency fingerprint and hands it to the planner; it is never authority. Decision 3's "any other method on such a path is still the terminal 404" now excepts that PATCH (doc 08 DA-136, amended).

**Amended (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8, ADR-43).** 022's authority derivation now has a second copy: migration 025's `tmpos_customer_search.m7a_customer_search` restates steps 1–6.5 of 022's `m7a_customer_get` statement for statement instead of calling it, because 022's read answers one customer. The two are held together by the database suites (`tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs`; `tests/db/customerSearch.integration.test.mjs` M7CS-06). 022's routine, its grants and its rollback are unchanged; 025 must be rolled back before 022.

## ADR-39 — The shop's Customer directory is a separate server-backed page: the session names the tenant, only a validated 201 confirms a create, and an unconfirmed create is re-sent only by hand on the same session (DECIDED — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5)

**Context.** After M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 the tenant entry served three customer routes in code, the list, the create and one customer by id (ADR-32, ADR-34, ADR-38), and the shop app called none of them. Its Customers screen (`src/components/Customers.tsx`) holds its records in the shop's local state, in the browser's memory only (they are lost when the page is reloaded), with contact details, loyalty, history and its own create and duplicate rules, while the server holds an id and a name per customer and nothing else (doc 05 §2). The owner brief for M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 asks for a clearly identified server-backed customer name directory in the shop:
- a customer created through the shop is persisted by the server, appears after a reload and in another authorized browser, and opens through the detail route;
- richer local-only workflows stay visibly distinct: no record merged by name, no server id assigned to a local record, no browser data erased, and nothing implying that contact or money fields are persisted;
- the shop's existing tenant session is reused, with no tenant, store, role or actor selector and no second authentication path;
- a malformed success is a failure, never an empty list or a confirmed creation;
- an uncertain create keeps its key and its exact body, is never reported as "not saved", and is never replayed automatically, after a tenant switch or with edited text; a same-name row confirms nothing, because names are not unique.

The brief allows a separate directory entry in the existing navigation where the existing screen cannot hold that distinction safely. The stage is shop-side: it changes no server route and adds no migration.

**Decision.**
1. **A separate page and sidebar entry.** "Customer directory" is one route, `/customers/directory/:id?` (`src/App.tsx`), under the same `AccessGuard feature="customers"` as the Customers screen, and one mounted component for the list and for one customer (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.tsx`), so the list survives opening a customer and coming back. The sidebar gains "Customer Directory" (`src/layouts/TenantLayout.tsx`), gated by the `customers` feature in the layout's accessible filter and its locked filter. Every existing entry is highlighted on an exact match, as before; only the Customer Directory entry is also highlighted on its sub-pages, so a customer's address highlights that entry and no other (`src/layouts/TenantLayout.customerDirectory.test.tsx` NAV-02).
   - **The local screen keeps its data model.** The Customers screen keeps every record and action it had. It gains only a banner, "Customer records on this page are held in this browser's memory only. They are lost when the page is reloaded and are not saved to the server.", and a link, "Open the Customer directory for customers saved on the server".
   - **Server rows stay in the page.** They live in the directory page's own state and nowhere else: never in the shop's local customer state, browser storage, a URL query or a log (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-43). A customer view shows its own answer, never a list row.
   - **Only an id and a name.** The page shows nothing else as server-backed, and says so: "Saved on the server for this store. Only the customer's name is stored here." and "Contact details, loyalty and history on the Customers page are held only in this browser's memory and are not linked to these records."
   - **Nothing is merged, imported, migrated or erased.** No record is matched by name, and no local record gains a server id.
2. **The session alone names the tenant.** The page uses the shop's existing tenant session client (ADR-36 decision 1) and runs its `ensure` before every request, reads included. The customers client (`src/tenant/customersClient.ts`) sends only `Accept` on the two GETs; the POST sends `Accept` and adds `Content-Type`, `X-TMPOS-CSRF: 1`, the session CSRF token as `X-TMPOS-Session-CSRF`, the `Idempotency-Key` and the body `{ name }`. No request names a tenant, store, role or actor, and none sends a `limit`. There is no second sign-in path: the Firebase ID token goes only to the existing session login. Requests are same-origin with credentials, `cache: 'no-store'` and `redirect: 'error'`. A 15 s timer covers obtaining the answer and reading a JSON body; a body that is not JSON is neither read nor cancelled (doc 08 DA-141). The client logs nothing, keeps no cache or storage, and never rejects.
3. **The connection rule.** A customer answer names no tenant. The session CSRF token is derived from the session identifier (`server/runtime/sessions.ts`), so token equality is the page's only witness of "same session, same tenant". A different token proves nothing about which tenant answers, and nothing is inferred from it.
   - The page keeps a connection as a counter and the token, in refs, and keeps the token beside an unconfirmed create in the in-memory hold store; the token is never put in React state, storage, a URL, the DOM or a log, and what React sees of an attempt is a view without the token (SCD-54). The first token fixes the connection.
   - A later different token starts a new one: reads in flight are cancelled; rows, the cursor, the customer view and notices are cleared; the rate-limit wait is reset; the reads start again under the note "The store connection changed. Check the list, then add again."; and an unconfirmed create is detached for good (decision 6).
   - A read answer applies only under the connection that started it, as the latest read started of its kind and, for a customer view, for the id the address still names. A superseded read is cancelled.
   - A window focus or a return to the tab runs the session check. While the token is unchanged it starts no list read, and it never sends a create (SCD-42); a changed token starts a new connection, which reads again (SCD-27). It never runs while a create is out (SCD-45) or during a rate-limit wait (SCD-60). A check that finds the sign-in no longer accepted clears the page; one the server cannot answer changes nothing (SCD-50).
   - One page per signed-in user, keyed by UID: a user switch mounts a fresh page, and one user's attempt is never shown to another.
   - Every page load that opens a server-backed shop screen (the role screen's server panel or this directory) signs in again, and a login ends the session presented to it. So such a screen opened in a second tab is a connection change for a page already open (doc 08 DA-137, DA-143).
4. **Reads.**
   - **Exact shapes.** A result is only a JSON body that matches the contract exactly: no extra key anywhere, at most 100 rows, every id a canonical lowercase UUID, every name a string, no id repeated within a page, `nextCursor` null or 23 base64url characters and never set on an empty page, and for one customer the id that was asked for (`src/tenant/customersClient.test.ts` CDC-03, CDC-07). Anything else, a static host's HTML 200 included, is a failure, never an empty directory. A body over 524,288 characters is not parsed.
   - **The cursor.** The server's `nextCursor` goes back verbatim as `cursor`; it is never decoded or built, and one outside its grammar is never sent. "Load more" appends rows in arrival order, the server's byte order (doc 08 DA-86), never re-sorted, each id once. A 400 to a cursor reloads once from the first page, with the note "The list changed on the server, so it was loaded again from the start." (doc 08 DA-84).
   - **401, or a sign-in the server refuses.** No rows, and "Reconnect".
   - **403.** No rows, and "This account may not view the customers saved on the server for this store." The page never falls back to the browser's records.
   - **A 401 or 403 clears both views.** Whichever read got it, the list or one customer, the rows, the cursor and the customer shown are all cleared (SCD-48). Reads already in flight are cancelled, so a late answer cannot repaint them (SCD-57).
   - **429.** The wait is the answer's `Retry-After` in whole seconds, held to 1–300, and 60 when it is absent or unreadable. The page's read, add and check buttons are disabled until it has passed. A later, shorter `Retry-After` never shortens a running wait, and a wait text states the wait running when it is written; it is not revised if a later answer lengthens the wait (SCD-51; doc 08 DA-142). No read starts during a wait: a customer opened then shows the wait and sends nothing (SCD-52). A first-page read that a wait blocked (after a confirmed create, a refused create, "Stop tracking" or a cursor reload) is remembered and runs once when the wait ends; it is a read, never a create (SCD-56).
   - **503, no answer, a malformed 200, a 404, or a first-page 400.** "The customer directory is unavailable right now." and "Try again". Rows this connection already confirmed stay only under the label "Last known list — could not refresh"; with none confirmed, no rows are shown (SCD-09).
   - **A failed "Load more" is a failed refresh.** It gets the same state as above: the rows stay under the last-known label, and "Try again" reads the first page. A 401 or 403 clears them (SCD-05, SCD-49).
   - **One customer.** A malformed id is never sent. A malformed id, a missing id and another tenant's id show one text, "No customer with this address is available in this store." A 200 shows the name and the id from that answer. When the server cannot be asked (an outage or a rate limit), an answer already shown for that id on this connection may stay under "Last known — could not refresh"; a 401 or 403 shows nothing.
5. **A create: one key per submitted draft, and only a validated 201 confirms.**
   - **Offered as a hint.** The form is shown when the browser's local role state holds Customers at Create or above (`hasPermission('customers')`); the server decides (doc 08 DA-140). "Add customer" is enabled only once this connection's list has loaded, with no unconfirmed attempt held and no rate-limit wait running.
   - **The name first.** A name the restated rule refuses (decision 7) gets an inline error, no request and no key.
   - **The session check.** A failed check sends nothing. A token other than this connection's starts a new connection and sends nothing.
   - **Held before it leaves.** Each submitted draft gets one new `Idempotency-Key`. The key, the exact body string (`JSON.stringify({ name })`), the normalized name, the session token and a monotonic send time are put in an in-memory hold store before the request leaves.
   - **The saved notice.** `Saved on the server: "<name>".` appears only on a 201 whose body is exactly `{ customer: { id, name } }` with a canonical id, whose `Location` is absent (doc 08 DA-134) or exactly `/api/v1/customers/<that id>`, and whose name equals the name sent (SCD-16). A fresh execution and a same-key replay confirm alike. `Location` is never followed: the notice links to the shop's own `/customers/directory/<id>`, and the first page is read again. The draft is cleared only when it still is the confirmed name (SCD-24 on the page's own connection, SCD-47 for the earlier-connection notice).
   - **Undecided.** A 200, any other 2xx, a 201 that fails those checks, a 409, a 5xx, any other status not listed under Refused, or no answer leaves the attempt unconfirmed: never "saved", and never "not saved".
   - **Refused.** A first send answered 401, 403, 400, 413, 415, 404, 422 or 429 ends the attempt with its own text and keeps the draft. That text is shown on the list and on a customer view alike (SCD-61). A 401 clears both views and offers "Reconnect"; a 403 reads the list again, once a running wait has ended (SCD-56).
6. **The unconfirmed attempt.** At most one per user, shown as a card apart from the form: `Not confirmed yet: "<name>". The server may have saved it.` No other customer can be added while it is held.
   - **"Check again".** Only by the user's action. The button is hidden when, as the card is drawn, the attempt is detached or older than 23 hours of monotonic time, a client cutoff under the server's 24 h key retention that a retry never extends. A click re-tests the cutoff and runs the session check first, and sends nothing if the cutoff has passed or the token is not the one the attempt was sent with. Otherwise it sends the same key and the same body string with the same session token. Only the confirming 201 settles it; every other answer leaves it unconfirmed, with a reason line.
   - **Detached, or too old.** No "Check again". The card says it was sent under an earlier connection, or too long ago, "and cannot be checked from here. The directory may show it, but a row with the same name is not proof."
   - **"Stop tracking".** Offered, with its warning, only while the attempt is shown as not confirmed: not while a first send or a "Check again" is out (SCD-16, SCD-23). A rate-limit wait does not disable it. The warning reads "Stop tracking does not cancel the request, and adding the same name again can create a second customer." It forgets the attempt and reads the first page again; during a rate-limit wait that read is held back until the wait ends (SCD-34, SCD-51; doc 08 DA-139).
   - **Never automatic.** No timer, focus event, connection change or remount sends a create. Editing the name field never changes what "Check again" sends.
   - **Reconciled by key, not by connection.** A late answer is never dropped. A confirmation for an attempt sent under an earlier connection shows `"<name>" was saved on the server under the earlier connection.`, with no link, and reads the first page again (SCD-32, SCD-47).
   - **An answer after the page has gone.** The hold store keeps a decided answer as a terminal outcome, saved or conclusively refused, until a mounted page that holds a session token shows it, once (SCD-46). A saved outcome is shown as the saved notice with its link when that page's token is the attempt's, and as the earlier-connection notice otherwise. A refused one is shown as `"<name>" was not added: the server refused that request.` Showing an outcome does not move keyboard focus (SCD-59). An undecided answer stays as the unconfirmed attempt (SCD-36). The page marks itself gone during the commit that removes it; no unit test can distinguish that from the cleanup that runs just after.
   - **In memory only.** The hold store and a kept outcome survive navigation inside the shop, not a reload (doc 08 DA-137).
7. **The name rule is restated in the shop, with a parity test.** `normalizeCustomerName` in `customersClient.ts` restates the server's rule (`server/runtime/customerCreate.ts`: trimmed, 1–200 characters, the refused control and direction characters, at least one visible character), because the server module reaches `node:buffer` and cannot be imported into the shipped bundle. `src/tenant/customersClient.test.ts` CDC-11 runs one corpus through both and requires identical answers, and CDC-12 does the same for the id rule. The server's check is unchanged and remains the authority.
8. **CI.** The quality job's limit rises from 20 to 30 minutes (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`). The browser step is now named "Shop role levels and customer directory in a real browser against the tenant entry and disposable PostgreSQL (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2, M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5)"; its 12-minute step limit is unchanged, and its file, `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs`, gains the directory scenarios SCB-01 to SCB-06.

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *Replacing the local Customers screen, or merging the server rows into it.* The local model holds fields and rules the server has no contract for. Replacing it would discard them or present them as saved, and merging by name would join unrelated records, since names are not unique.
- (b) *Persisting the unconfirmed attempt in browser storage.* A reloaded page signs in again and holds another session, so under decision 6 it could not re-send the attempt anyway, and persisting it would put a customer name and a key in browser storage.
- (c) *Binding the customer GETs to the checked session by a header.* It needs the server to require the session CSRF token on a read, a server change outside this stage. Without it the consequence is display-only, because every create carries the token (doc 08 DA-138).
- (d) *Re-sending an unconfirmed create after the session changed, by "re-qualifying" it from a list row* (the design's first draft). The GETs are not bound to the checked session, so such a row could come from another tenant's session, and the re-send would create the customer there. Removed in design review: after a token change the attempt is detached for good.
- (e) *Treating a same-name row as confirmation.* Names need not be unique (doc 05 §2), so a row with the name proves nothing about this key.
- (f) *Reading the response body as a stream, to stop at the size cap before the whole body is buffered.* Not adopted: the customers client follows the pattern of the shop's existing tenant clients, its requests are same-origin, and a read is bounded by the 15 s request timer. The limit of that pattern is recorded (doc 08 DA-141).

**Consequences.**
- **Nothing closes.** G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION stay OPEN, and DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
  - G-PERSIST is not claimed: these routes store only a customer's name, nothing has been deployed or run against a managed database, and the Customers screen's records are held only in the browser's memory, not linked to the server's (DA-144).
  - The tenant entry is not deployed, and the shop build and `/api/v1/*` must be served on one origin, the tenant host (DA-107). Where no tenant server answers, the page shows "The customer directory is unavailable right now." and no rows.
- **Supersedes.** ADR-36's "the shop app does not call them" and doc 09's P3 and R2 statements that the shop does not call the customer routes, and P4 Part 2's "Next product step", are superseded. ADR-32's and ADR-34's "the shop app still keeps (its) customers in the browser" are narrowed to the Customers screen's own records (amendments above).
- **Register.** DA-84 and DA-107 are amended. DA-137 to DA-146 are new:
  - an unconfirmed create is lost on a reload, and after a session change it can no longer be checked from the shop (DA-137);
  - the customer GETs are not bound to the session the page just checked (DA-138);
  - "Stop tracking" forgets an unconfirmed create (DA-139);
  - the add form is offered from the browser's local role state (DA-140);
  - the shop's tenant clients check their response-size cap only after the whole body is buffered (DA-141);
  - a rate-limit wait is forgotten on a connection change or a re-opened page (DA-142);
  - every page load that opens a server-backed shop screen signs in again, against the login limit (DA-143);
  - the Customers screen's records and the server directory are not linked, and nothing is migrated (DA-144);
  - locked sidebar items carry no "unavailable" state for assistive technology, a pre-existing finding (DA-145);
  - the entry-point suite's bare-import case has a 5 s limit that a cold start on the development host can exceed, a pre-existing test outside this stage's paths (DA-146).
- **Evidence.** Local sockets, a synthetic identity, the Firestore emulator and disposable PostgreSQL only:
  - `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` (SCD-00 to SCD-61), `src/tenant/customersClient.test.ts` (CDC-01 to CDC-12), `src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.test.ts` (TSC-13, TSC-14), `src/components/Customers.localBanner.test.tsx` (LCB-01, LCB-02) and `src/layouts/TenantLayout.customerDirectory.test.tsx` (NAV-01 to NAV-04);
  - `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SCB-01 to SCB-06, in a real browser against the tenant entry: the create with its reload, second-account read and detail by click and by address; one not-found text for another tenant's, an unknown and a malformed id; paging; a permission narrowed while the page is open; a reply lost after the commit; and a held attempt whose grant moved;
  - entry witness: on a build of the entry commit `5b4259e` SCB-01 fails, because the shop sends no `POST /api/v1/customers`; on the final bytes it passes.

  Counts and exits: local gate on the `e590db2` bytes: frontend lane 24 files and 510 cases; shop browser gate 20/20 under the Firestore emulator; disposable-database gate 27 suites 536/536; shared-service CI replay 27 steps 496/496; 0 sentinel dials. The node lane (160 suites) was 158/160 and 159/160 on its first two runs and 160/160 on the third (doc 08 DA-146). `build:check` and the admin browser gate (41/41) passed from a clean export of the candidate; in the working tree the production build did not finish, and those runs are not counted as passed. Migrations 001–022 and the frozen operator paths are byte-identical to the entry commit (sha256). Commit `e590db2`; exact-commit CI: run 36722708353, green on attempt 1. Code-only: nothing is deployed, and no managed database was touched.

**Amended (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1, ADR-40).** Decision 2's statement that the customers client "sends only `Accept` on the two GETs" is superseded: both GETs now also carry the session CSRF token as `X-TMPOS-Session-CSRF`, and the server refuses a read whose cookie is another session's (doc 08 DA-138, closed in code). Decision 3's last bullet is superseded: a page load that opens a server-backed shop screen now reuses the signed-in user's session instead of signing in again, so a second tab no longer changes the connection of a page already open (DA-143, closed in code). Alternative (c) is now taken, and alternative (b)'s premise that "a reloaded page signs in again and holds another session" no longer holds, though the hold store is still in memory only (DA-137, amended). Decision 6 is unchanged: after any token change an unconfirmed create is detached for good.

**Amended (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8, ADR-43).** The directory page's list gains a name search form; the server matches, and the page still never filters, ranks or inserts a row itself. After a confirmed create or edit, the list reloads from its first page under the applied search, if any, so a created customer appears in a search's results only when the server returns it; a confirmed edit first puts the confirmed name in the edited customer's shown row, so a failed reload never shows the name from before the edit. Search actions take the list's reads and the plain session step: no sign-in after a confirmed end, only Reconnect's *(wording corrected in M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10; it first read "never a sign-in")*.

## ADR-40 — A customer read names the session its page checked, and a page load reuses the signed-in user's own session (DECIDED — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1)

**Context.** ADR-39 left two recorded defects in the shop's Customer directory. The list and detail GETs carried no session token, so they were answered under whichever session cookie the browser held when they arrived, not necessarily the one the page had just checked (doc 08 DA-138). And the shop's session client remembered a session only in the page's memory, so every page load that opened a server-backed shop screen signed in again, against a tenant login limit of 5 per account per 5 minutes, and each login ended the session the browser presented (DA-143). The entry witnesses on `f93215b` showed both:
- over HTTP (`tests/db/customerDirectory.integration.test.mjs` RB-01 to RB-06), a list read with tenant B's cookie was answered 200 with B's 50 rows whether it carried A's session CSRF token, no token, two token lines or a malformed one; a cursor page was answered 200, and so was a detail read of B's customer under A's token; and the session 200s named no subject;
- in a real browser (`tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SCB-07), one owner's first open, reloads, an in-app reopen, a load showing both server consumers and a second tab sent 12 logins in one case: five were answered 200, and from the sixth load on every load was answered 429 and showed no rows; the mint ledger grew by five;
- and (SCB-08) page A's list read, released with tenant B's session cookie after the page's session check, was answered 200, and B's three ids were shown on A's page as A's; the detail read of A's own customer was answered 404.

The owner brief for M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 asks for both corrections as one bounded product change:
- the reads bound on the server to the session the page checked, by a witness the server compares with the session authenticated for that request and never uses as authority;
- a reload or reopen that reuses a valid session of the same identity instead of signing in again, while an expired or revoked session reconnects through the established login, an outage stays an outage, and a session another identity left in the browser is never accepted merely because it is valid;
- no second bearer mechanism, no secret exposed to script, and no new dependency, secret, endpoint or migration;
- ADR-39's treatment of an uncertain create unchanged.

What "the session token" is, from the stage's inventory of the mechanism:
- **The cookie.** The HttpOnly `__Host-tmpos_tenant_session` cookie holds the session identifier. Script never reads it.
- **The session CSRF token.** HMAC-SHA256 keyed by the session identifier over `tmpos-session-csrf:v1:tenant`, 43 base64url characters (`server/runtime/sessions.ts`). The browser receives it only in the 200 of `GET /api/v1/session` and of the login. A new session has a new token, and the token alone grants nothing.
- **The tenant.** The session record holds the verified principal and no tenant. Admission and the authorizer re-derive the tenant on every request from the user's one tenant grant, and a moved grant ends the old session on its next request (401; `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SRL-10). A live session is therefore always its user's current tenant's. The shop holds no client tenant id.

**Decision.**
1. **A read bound to its session carries the session CSRF token.** In the enforced chain (`server/runtime/app.ts`), a route that declares `sessionBinding: 'required'` now requires `X-TMPOS-Session-CSRF` equal to the presenting session's token on its GET, as every unsafe method already did. Exactly two routes declare it (`server/runtime/customerDirectory.ts`): `GET /api/v1/customers`, cursor pages included, and `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}`.
   - **Refusal.** The existing session-CSRF check: a missing header is `csrf_session_missing`, two header lines are `csrf_session_duplicate`, and a malformed token or any other session's is `csrf_session_invalid`. The answer is always `403 { error: 'forbidden', requestId }`; the reason is in the request log only.
   - **Order.** Cookie authentication first, so a missing, ended or revoked session is still 401. Then the token, then the authorizer, the port and any cursor. A refused read reaches no customer row and slides no idle window.
   - **A witness, never authority.** The token is compared, in fixed time, with the one derived from the session the cookie presents. It never selects a tenant, principal or permission, and the read still runs on that session's trusted context and store key (ADR-33). It travels only in a header, never in a URL.
   - **Unchanged.** Under the right token the answers are those of ADR-32 and ADR-38, the identical 404 for a foreign or missing id included. `GET /api/v1/session`, the login, the logout and `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` are unchanged, and the last stays unbound (doc 08 DA-147).
2. **The tenant session names its subject.** On the tenant boundary only, the 200 of `GET /api/v1/session` and of `POST /api/v1/session/login` is `{ status: 'active', csrfToken, subject }`. `subject` is `sessionSubjectOf(principal)`: SHA-256 in base64url over `tmpos-session-subject:v1`, the provider and the UID, each on its own line, of the session's own verified principal. The label keeps it distinct from the principal's storage key (`principalKeyOf`). It names no tenant, grants nothing and is never taken as input. The administrative boundary's body is unchanged, `{ status, csrfToken }`.
3. **The shop's reads send the token its check just answered.** `customersClient.list(cursor, csrf, signal)` and `detail(id, csrf, signal)` put the token `ensure` just answered in `X-TMPOS-Session-CSRF` (`src/tenant/customersClient.ts`). The server gives a read under a replaced cookie the same 403 as a permission refusal, so on the directory page (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.tsx`) a read answered 403 is followed by one session check. That check is a fresh session read sent after the 403 (`ensure(uid, …, { fresh: true })`): it never takes the answer of a session read already in flight before the refusal, and if another user's sign-in, a logout or a reset came in between, it sends nothing and answers unavailable.
   - another token starts a new connection (ADR-39 decision 3): the page clears, reads again under "The store connection changed. Check the list, then add again." and detaches an unconfirmed create;
   - the same token leaves the refusal standing: "This account may not view the customers saved on the server for this store.";
   - a refused check (the session ended) replaces the read's refusal with its own;
   - a check that cannot decide (an outage, a rate limit) leaves the read's own refusal: both views are cleared as for any refusal, and a rate limit's wait still runs. A refused read never falls back to last-known rows or a last-known customer.
4. **A page load reuses the signed-in user's session.** `tenantSessionClient.ensure(uid)` always reads `GET /api/v1/session` first (`src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.ts`):
   - **The same user.** A 200 whose `subject` equals the digest of `uid` is reused with its current token: no ID token is asked for and no login is sent. The shop recomputes the digest from the signed-in Firebase UID with `crypto.subtle` (provider `firebase`) and never sends it.
   - **No session, or another identity's.** A 401, or a 200 naming another identity (a session a previous user left in this browser), leads to the login, which ends the session presented to it (the server's existing rule). The confirming session read must name this user too; one that names someone else confirms nothing.
   - **Anything else.** A 429, a 5xx, no answer, a 403, a malformed 200, a 200 without a well-formed subject, or a digest that cannot be computed ends the attempt as rate-limited or unavailable, with no login and no loop.
   - **Coordination.** Concurrent `ensure` calls for one UID still share one attempt, and a reset still supersedes older work, so a late session read from an earlier initialization answers nothing. The role screen's server panel and the directory share this client. The shop's auth-state handling is unchanged: a Firebase sign-out or user switch still logs the previous user's session out first (ADR-36 decision 1).
5. **Unchanged.** ADR-39 decision 6: an unconfirmed create is detached for good after any token change, never re-sent automatically, and kept in memory only. The idempotency and rate-limit policies are unchanged, so the login's account limit still applies to real sign-ins. So are the permissions, the tenant entry's ten routes and migrations 001–022. No dependency, secret or endpoint is added; one existing transitive dependency is updated for a security advisory (below).

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *A session read just before or after each customer read, as the binding.* The cookie can still change between that read and the request, so it proves nothing about the request itself.
- (b) *Client cancellation and connection checks alone.* They stop a late answer from repainting the page, but not the server from answering under another session. The brief requires the server's refusal.
- (c) *A separate read witness, or a tenant witness on the session.* The session CSRF token is already a per-session witness, compared in fixed time and already carried by the create. A second token would be a second mechanism, and a tenant witness is not needed while a live session is always its user's current tenant's.
- (d) *Returning the UID, or reusing any valid session.* A raw UID adds nothing a labelled digest does not. Reusing a session because it is valid would let one person's session answer for another in a shared browser.
- (e) *Keeping the token or the UID in browser storage to skip the session read.* It would put the session token in storage, against ADR-39 decision 3, and a stored value cannot tell whether the cookie still belongs to that user; the session read can.
- (f) *Raising the login's account limit.* Every load's login would still end the session other tabs hold (DA-137).
- (g) *Binding `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` as well.* Outside the brief. Its answer names its tenant, which the role panel already compares (DA-147).

**Consequences.**
- **Closed in code.** DA-138 and DA-143. Nothing is deployed. G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION stay OPEN, and DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
- **Behaviour on change.**
  - *Identity switch.* The shop logs the previous user's session out first. A session a previous identity left in the browser is never reused: this user signs in, and that login ends it.
  - *Tenant switch.* A moved grant ends the old session, so the next session read is 401 and the page signs in again. The new session has a new token, so the page starts a new connection and an unconfirmed create is detached.
  - *Expiry or revocation.* A 401, then a sign-in through the established login and its limits.
  - *Reload, reopen or second tab.* The same session and token, usually. An unconfirmed create held by an open page therefore stays checkable when the shop is opened in another tab. A reload still loses it, because the hold is in memory only (DA-137).
  - *Session lifetime.* A session now lasts across page loads until a logout, its 30-minute idle or 8-hour absolute limit (DA-127) or its revocation. Before, each page load's login replaced it after re-verifying a fresh ID token (DA-149).
  - *Recovery after a refused read.* When a read was refused because the cookie was another identity's, the page's session check signs its own user in again, and that login ends the other session in this browser (SCB-08). A residual, not a defect: with one shared Firebase sign-in per browser both tabs are the same identity, and the entry stage signed in on every load anyway.
- **Supersedes.** ADR-39 decision 2's "sends only `Accept` on the two GETs", decision 3's last bullet and alternative (c); ADR-39 alternative (b)'s premise no longer holds. ADR-36 decision 1's reuse rule is restated (amendments above).
- **Register.** DA-138 and DA-143 are closed in code, and DA-137 is amended. DA-147 to DA-149 are new:
  - `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` is not bound to the session the role panel checked (DA-147);
  - the session subject is a stable per-user identifier that the session holder can read (DA-148);
  - reuse rests on the shop's digest of the Firebase UID, and a reused session is not re-verified with a fresh ID token (DA-149).
- **Evidence.** Local sockets, synthetic identities, the Firestore emulator and disposable PostgreSQL only:
  - `tests/db/customerDirectory.integration.test.mjs` RB-01 to RB-06: B's cookie with A's token, no token, two token lines and malformed tokens on the list, a cursor page and a detail read are each 403 with no `customers` key; B's own token reads B's rows only; a foreign id under the right token is still 404; both session 200s carry the session's own subject, and two users' differ;
  - `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SCB-07 (one login, one session row and one mint row across the first open, six reloads, an in-app reopen, a load showing both server consumers and a second tab, with no 429, a session read before every first customer read and the rows on every load) and SCB-08 (a list read and a detail read, each released with B's cookie after the page's check: 403 with no customers, no B id or name in any answer to page A or in its DOM, and the page ends on A's rows under a new A session);
  - `src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.test.ts` TSC-15 to TSC-24 (the subject is equal on the client, the server and an independent digest; seven fresh loads send seven session reads and no login; another identity's session is never reused; a late session read from an earlier initialization is dropped; a digest failure is unavailable, with no login; a fresh check waits for a session read in flight and reads again, and after another user's sign-in or a reset sends nothing; a user switch or reset while the digest is computed keeps nothing; a digest that cannot be made after a 401 sends no login; a fresh check for a user the client has moved on from sends nothing and ends no other attempt) and TSC-01 to TSC-14 adapted; `src/tenant/customersClient.test.ts` CDC-13; `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-62 to SCD-72 (a swapped list or detail read recovers under a new connection; a real refusal stays one check; a refused read whose check cannot decide shows nothing, and a rate limit's wait still runs; a refused "Load more" or detail read under the same token is one check; three remounts over one session send one login; a first session read answered 429 or 503 sends no login; the check after a refusal is a new session read, never one already in flight), with SCD-00, SCD-01 and SCD-38 adapted; `src/tenant/ServerCustomerLevels.test.tsx` SCL-53 (three reopened role panels over one session send one login) and SCL-10 adapted; and the server suites `server/runtime/customerDirectory.test.ts` and `server/runtime/sessions.test.ts`;
  - entry witness: on the entry commit `f93215b` RB-01 to RB-04 and RB-06, SCB-07 and SCB-08 fail as described above; on the final bytes they pass.

- **Dependency advisory (separate commit, before the code).** Two `@grpc/grpc-js` advisories, GHSA-m9gg-hp2v-232j (High) and GHSA-f596-whhp-79r4 (Low), both fixed in 1.13.6 and 1.14.5 (published 2026-09-17), reached the advisory database on 2026-09-30, after P5's final runs, so the dependency audit would have failed for any commit. All three copies now resolve to one `@grpc/grpc-js@1.14.5` with its own `@grpc/proto-loader@0.8.1`; the Firestore client keeps `@grpc/proto-loader@0.7.15` from its declared range. The two under `google-gax` move from 1.14.4 within their declared ranges (`^1.10.9`, `^1.12.6`). The Firestore client SDK's copy moves by its `package.json` override from 1.9.16 to 1.14.5. **That override is outside `@firebase/firestore`'s declared range `~1.9.0`**: this is an out-of-range compatibility change, not a patch update, because no fixed 1.9.x release exists. The browser bundle does not include grpc-js. The override's selector is unversioned, as it already was for 1.9.16, so a later `@firebase/firestore` release would also receive 1.14.5 until the override is revisited. Firestore's client header still reports `grpc/1.9.15`; that is telemetry only. The compatibility evidence is the Firestore emulator suite, the shop browser gate and the admin console containment suite, all run on a clean install of the updated lockfile: Firestore emulator rules suite 41/41, shop browser gate 22/22, admin console containment suite 41/41 and the full verification, every step exit 0 except the raw `npm audit` exit, which the dependency-audit reconciliation (exit 0) decides. Production audit: 0 Critical and 0 High (before the change, 4 High packages, all from the one High advisory). The full tree keeps its 4 recorded development-only High packages. No manifest exception was added, and the audit thresholds are unchanged. Commit `df3fc4e`, exact-commit CI 36789768621 green on all 62 steps.

  Counts and exits: 160/160 main suites, 522/522 frontend tests, 500 database tests across the replay with 0 ambient-database dials, shop browser 22/22, admin console 41/41, Firestore emulator 41/41; every verification step exit 0 except the raw `npm audit` exit, which the dependency-audit reconciliation (exit 0) decides. Migrations 001–022 and the frozen operator paths are byte-identical to the entry commit (sha256): `git diff` of migrations 001–022 is 0 bytes, and the entry sha256 manifests for the migrations and the frozen operator paths both check exit 0. Commit `5479d1e` (after the dependency commit `df3fc4e`); exact-commit CI: 36789768621 (`df3fc4e`) and 36791433899 (`5479d1e`), green on every step. Code-only: nothing is deployed, and no managed database was touched.

**Amended (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6, ADR-41).** Decision 1's "Exactly two routes declare it" now reads three: `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile` declares `sessionBinding: 'required'` too. Decision 4's "A 401 … leads to the login" and the Consequences' *Expiry or revocation* ("A 401, then a sign-in through the established login and its limits") are narrowed: in a page whose session client has already confirmed this user's session, a 401 is `ended`, and the login is sent only when the user presses Reconnect. A fresh page's first 401 still leads to the login.

## ADR-41 — Customer contact records: an optional phone and email and a database-written creation time, read only through a session-bound profile route; and an ended session waits for the user's Reconnect (DECIDED — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6)

**Context.** After M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 the server held a customer's id, tenant and name only (017), and the shop's Customer directory showed nothing else; the browser-local Customers screen held contact details only in memory (doc 08 DA-144). The owner brief for M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 asks:
- for an optional phone and email and a trustworthy creation time on server customers, created through the existing idempotent create and read from another authorized browser, with the tenant from the verified session only;
- that existing ids, names and name-only contracts stay exactly as they are (the list and detail `{ id, name }`, the name-only create's bytes, fingerprint and sealed 201 with `Location`), and that a legacy customer's unknown creation time be shown as unknown, never as the migration's time;
- that local customer records stay untouched, with no import and no links;
- that contacts stay out of logs, audit metadata and idempotency diagnostics, and that retention and deletion stay an explicit pre-live decision;
- and that the reported automatic reconnect be checked against P5-R1's brief. It was not compliant: after an expired or revoked session the shop's session client sent a login on its own at the next session step, with no user action (P5-R1's *Expiry or revocation* consequence), and the tests that encoded that are changed (TSC-02 among them).

**Decision.**
1. **Three server-owned fields on 017's table (migration 023).** `phone` and `email`, text with collation `"C"`, NULL when not provided, and `created_at` (timestamptz). `created_at` is added with no default, so every existing row keeps NULL; the default `now()` is set separately and applies only to rows inserted afterwards. Named CHECKs `customer_phone_chk` and `customer_email_chk` restate the contact rules. No index, no uniqueness, no matching or merging, no verification, consent or ownership semantics. Ids, names, tenants and the page order are untouched.
2. **One contact rule, at least as strict in the runtime and the shop as in the database.** Omitted, `null`, or blank after `trim()` is NULL; another non-string is 400. A value is stored trimmed and otherwise as typed, with no C0 control (NUL included), DEL, C1 control, U+2028/U+2029, lone surrogate or Bidi_Control character. Phone: 1–64 scalars with an ASCII digit. Email: 1–254 scalars, no White_Space anywhere, exactly one `@` between non-empty parts. The runtime (`server/runtime/customerDirectory.ts`), the shop client (`src/tenant/customersClient.ts`, with a parity test) and 023 (its CHECKs and its create's own check, which raises 22023 naming the field, never the value) agree; NUL and lone surrogates are refused by the runtime only (doc 08 DA-151).
3. **The create keeps its contract and gains two optional keys.** Body keys `name`, `phone`, `email`, at most 8,192 bytes; the 201 stays `{ customer: { id, name } }` with `Location`. The fingerprint stays the keyed hash of the exact body bytes: a name-only body keeps its fingerprint, a changed contact under one key is `422 idempotency_key_reused`, `IDEMPOTENCY_CONTRACT_VERSION` is unchanged, and earlier sealed completions replay as sealed. Still one transaction (customer, audit record with metadata `{}`, completion); no outbox event. DA-137 is unchanged.
4. **One source of authority per routine.** 023's `tmpos_customer_contact.m7a_contact_create` judges the contacts, then calls 020's `tmpos_role.m7a_customer_create` as its owner for the whole decision and the insert, then writes the contacts onto exactly that row in exactly the tenant 020 derived. 023's `m7a_contact_profile` calls 022's `m7a_customer_get` for every decision and the point read, then reads that row's contacts and creation time on the same snapshot. Both are `SECURITY DEFINER`, pinned to `search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp`, with `row_security = off`. `tmpos_app` gets USAGE and EXECUTE on them only and loses EXECUTE on 020's create; it holds no table privilege. The startup preflight requires exactly 001–023 and these grants.
