5. **Contacts are read on one route only.** `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile` answers `200 { customer: { id, name, phone, email, createdAt } }` (`createdAt` in `toISOString()` UTC form or `null`), under exactly the detail read's policy (`view_customers`, tenant audience, trusted context, session binding, no body, no idempotency, no recent sign-in) and in its order of refusals; missing and foreign ids are the same 404. The runtime validates the database's answer and treats anything out of shape as a 503. The router admits it as its second and last template, by name. The list and the detail read stay `{ id, name }`. No audit record (the list's owner decision).
6. **Legacy creation times stay unknown.** A customer created before 023 answers `createdAt: null`, and the shop shows "Not recorded". No backfill (doc 08 DA-152).
7. **The rollback never discards customer data.** 023's down refuses, dropping nothing, while any customer holds a phone, an email or a creation time; otherwise it returns exactly the 001–022 database, 020's grant included.
8. **The shop's contact interface (commit `0f957c3`).** The add form marks Name required and Phone and Email optional, with accessible field feedback; invalid input sends nothing. A blank contact is left out of the body, so a name-only create keeps its exact bytes. A customer's page reads the profile route and shows "Not provided" for an absent contact and "Not recorded" for an unknown creation time; a contact is shown nowhere else. A create confirmed by its validated 201 stays confirmed if the following profile read fails. A session or tenant change clears the profile. The Customers screen is untouched, and nothing is imported or linked.
9. **An ended session waits for Reconnect (commit `52f17d7`).** `tenantSessionClient.ensure(uid, idToken, { fresh?, reconnect? })` remembers, in the page's memory only, the user whose session it confirmed in this page's life. For that user a session read answered 401 is `ended`: no ID token, no login, on every later call, until a read finds a valid session of that user again (reused with no login) or the caller passes `reconnect`. Only the directory's two Reconnect buttons and the role panel's Reconnect pass it. Unchanged: a fresh page's first 401 logs in; another identity's session still logs this user in; an outage, a 429 or a malformed answer logs nothing in; an unconfirmed create is never re-sent during recovery.

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *A side table for contacts.* A contact is a fact about one customer, read and written with it; a side table needs its own tenant key, rollback and inventory for the same row, and 017–022's schemas pin their own inventories.
- (b) *Contacts on the list or the detail read.* It would change two name-only contracts and put personal data on every page of the list. One route reads them.
- (c) *Stamping existing rows with the migration's time, or a default on `ADD COLUMN`.* It would label the migration's time a creation time.
- (d) *Re-deriving the authority inside 023's routines.* Two copies of 020's and 022's logic could disagree; delegation keeps one source.
- (e) *A new idempotency contract version or a parsed-body fingerprint.* It would change every name-only fingerprint; the byte fingerprint already refuses a changed contact.
- (f) *Storing a "session ended" marker in browser storage, so a reload also waits.* It would put session state in storage, and the server's 401 cannot confirm what ended. Recorded instead (doc 08 DA-150).
- (g) *A separate permission for contact details.* Not in the brief; recorded for the owner (DA-153).

**Consequences.**
- **Closed.** Nothing. G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY stay OPEN; DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. Nothing is deployed.
- **Behaviour on change.** *Expiry or revocation in an open page:* both views cleared and "Reconnect"; one login only on the click. *Reload with an expired cookie:* a fresh page; one login on first load (DA-150). *Another tab's valid session of the same user:* reused with no login. *Tenant or identity change:* contact data, profile, cursors and notices are cleared, as for every other read.
- **Supersedes.** ADR-34's request body; ADR-35's statement that `tmpos_app` executes 020's create; ADR-38 decision 1's single template; ADR-40 decision 1's "exactly two routes" and its *Expiry or revocation* consequence (amendments above).
- **Register.** DA-150 to DA-155 are new; DA-118, DA-136, DA-144 and DA-147 are amended (none closed); DA-137, DA-148 and DA-149 are unchanged.
- **Evidence.** Disposable PostgreSQL, local sockets, synthetic identities and synthetic contacts only (for example `Ada Lovelace`, `+1 555 0100`, `ada@example.test`):
  - `tests/db/customerContact.integration.test.mjs` M7CN-01 to M7CN-09, run by a new CI step: apply and postcondition; the upgrade over populated legacy rows (ids, names, tenants and page order kept, contacts and `created_at` NULL); the login's grants; a create storing the contacts and the creating transaction's `now()`; every refused contact class; parity with 020's refusals; the profile read, its legacy NULL and its parity with 022; and the rollback refusing while data exists;
  - `tests/db/roleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M7RLH-08 now witnesses DA-118 on 023's routine;
  - reconnect: `src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.test.ts` TSC-25 to TSC-31, `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-73 to SCD-82, `src/tenant/ServerCustomerLevels.test.tsx` SCL-54 and SCL-55, and the browser scenario SCB-09 of `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs`;
  - the contact interface (commit `0f957c3`): SCD-83 to SCD-92, `src/tenant/customersClient.test.ts` CDC-14 and CDC-15, and the browser scenario SCB-10.

  Exact-commit CI: 36859957536 (`52f17d7`), green on attempt 2 (attempt 1 failed only M6R-PERF, the recorded rate-limiter load flake; that commit changes no server code), and 36873703631 (`77508c1`), green on attempt 1. 36877615589 (`0f957c3`), green on attempt 1. Code-only: nothing is deployed, and no managed database was touched.

**Amended (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, ADR-42).** Decision 4's grants are narrowed: migration 024 revokes `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE on 023's `m7a_contact_profile`, and the runtime reads the profile through 024's `tmpos_customer_edit.m7a_customer_profile`, which delegates to it and adds the row version on the same snapshot; 023's create is unchanged. Decision 5's "second and last template" is superseded: the router admits a third, `PATCH /api/v1/customers/{id}`, and the profile 200 carries the version as a strong `ETag`, its JSON body unchanged. Decision 7 stands, and the rollback order becomes 024, then 023, 022, 021, 020; 024's own rollback refuses while any customer has been edited or any audit record names a customer.

## ADR-42 — Editing a saved customer: a versioned, idempotent PATCH at Customers Edit, the version as the profile's strong ETag, the edited customer as the audit subject, and a rollback that never resets a version (DECIDED — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7)

**Context.** After M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 a server customer held a name, an optional phone and email and a creation time, created through the idempotent create and read through the profile route, and nothing could change them (doc 05 §2). The owner brief for M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 asks:
- that an authorized shop user correct a saved customer's name, phone and email from that customer's page and see the change after a reload, under Customers at Edit or above, honouring owner-edited levels, with no role default changed;
- that a stale editor never overwrite somebody else's committed change (a database row version), and that the change, its audit record and its idempotency completion commit together or not at all;
- a body of `expectedVersion` with optional `name`, `phone` and `email`, where omitted means unchanged and `null` or blank clears a contact; a sealed `write_conflict` for a stale version, one 404 for missing and foreign ids, a no-op that writes nothing, a replay before evaluation and a 422 for a changed request under one key;
- the version exposed through the existing profile read without changing its JSON;
- an audit record naming the actor, the tenant and the target customer, with the changed field names if the writer supports them and never a value;
- a rollback that neither discards contact data nor resets versions;
- a shop that shows "Saved" only on a validated answer, explains a conflict and lets the user review it, never re-sends an uncertain edit on its own, keeps pending edits in memory only, and keeps P6's Reconnect;
- that the create, list, detail and profile contracts, historical fingerprints and replays, legacy creation times and browser-only records stay as they are.

**Decision.**
1. **The permission.** `edit_customers`: the Customers domain at Edit or above, decided by the materializer and evaluator that decide `view_customers` and `create_customers`, on the role's effective level. No role default changes: `store_owner` and `manager` hold it, `technician` and `sales_staff` do not. A `read_only` limitation denies it. The load-time threshold set admits exactly View, Create and Edit. The authorizer decides it, the revalidator decides it again in the committing transaction, and 024's routine decides Edit again on the locked stored level.
2. **The command.** `PATCH /api/v1/customers/{id}` (`server/runtime/customerUpdate.ts`): tenant audience, trusted context, `Idempotency-Key` required, no recent sign-in, at most 8,192 bytes, no outbox event; the route table's third template and its only templated command. Body keys: `expectedVersion` (1 to 2^53 − 2) and `name` required; `phone` and `email` optional (omitted keeps, `null` or blank clears, a value is normalized by the create's rule); anything else is 400. The answers: `200 { customer: { id, name }, version, changed }`; a stale version (a stale no-op included) the sealed `409 {"error":"write_conflict"}`; a missing or foreign id the same unsealed 404; an outage or a lost COMMIT answer the bounded 503.
3. **Name is required in every edit, a stated deviation from the brief.** The kernel seals the 200 from the plan before the commit, and only values cross the store port, so a planner cannot know a stored name it was not sent. Requiring `name` keeps the answer exactly the stored name with no kernel change; a later kernel change may relax it additively (doc 08 DA-156).
4. **A no-op is a sealed 200 that changes no customer.** At the matching version with every target already stored, the answer is `changed: false` at the same version: no customer change, no version move, no audit record, no event. Its sealed response is recorded as the key's idempotency completion in that transaction, while the row is still locked, so a retry replays it. *(Wording corrected at M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8, ADR-43: this decision said the no-op "writes nothing"; it writes its completion. The behaviour is unchanged.)*
5. **The target is bound into the key.** The fingerprint is contract version 2's array with the canonical target appended after the body. A literal route's fingerprint keeps its bytes, so `IDEMPOTENCY_CONTRACT_VERSION` stays 2 and the create's pinned vector is unchanged. The same key with another body or another customer is 422. A completed edit's retry replays before anything is evaluated again, under the current session and authorization. The target must also have the outbox's aggregate-id shape (DA-157). *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P2, [ADR-48](#adr-48--editing-a-saved-store-invoice-draft-at-invoices-edit-versioned-and-idempotent-with-sealed-conflicts-and-no-ops-decided--m7b-invoices-p2): a store template with `{id}` binds the path's draft id as its target the same way; the save, which has no `{id}`, keeps binding the store, and no fingerprint of an earlier route changes.)*
6. **The version (migration 024).** `tmpos_customer.customer.row_version`: bigint, not null, constant default 1, CHECK 1 to 9007199254740991, no trigger. Only `tmpos_customer_edit.m7a_customer_update` moves it, by exactly one, in the statement that writes the values, after comparing it under the row lock and before comparing any value. Existing rows read 1 and are not rewritten; creation times are untouched.
7. **The version's transport.** The profile 200 carries `ETag: "<version>"`, a strong validator from the same routine call as the values: 024's `m7a_customer_profile`, which delegates to 023's read on one snapshot. The JSON body is byte-identical to P6's. No conditional request is processed, so the 200 never becomes a 304, and `no-store` stays. The shop treats a missing, weak or malformed ETag, or a 304, as unavailable and never guesses a version.
8. **The audit subject.** `public.audit_event.subject_customer_id` (uuid, nullable, partial index), written only by the command transaction, from the aggregate the mutator locked, for this command only. Action `tenant.customer.update`, permission `edit_customers`, metadata `{}`: the changed field names are not recorded (DA-158), and no value is.
9. **The database routine.** `m7a_customer_update` judges every argument first and requires READ COMMITTED. It restates 020's authority derivation at Edit, with the expected tenant as a precondition. Then it locks the row FOR UPDATE, reads the clock again, compares the version, and answers `not_found`, `conflict`, `unchanged` or `applied`; any authority refusal is no row. `tmpos_app` gets USAGE and EXECUTE on the two routines, loses EXECUTE on 023's profile read, and holds no table or column privilege. The startup preflight requires exactly 001–024 and these grants.
10. **The rollback never resets a version or erases a subject.** 024's down refuses, dropping nothing: while any `row_version` is above 1 or any audit record names a customer; while any column grant exists on the customer table or reaches `tmpos_app`; outside READ COMMITTED; and when 024 or 023's profile read is missing. Otherwise it returns exactly the 001–023 database.
11. **The shop's edit (commit `5253304`).** Edit is offered on a confirmed, versioned profile while the local role hint holds Customers at Edit or above (DA-140). One body per attempt, under a new key, after the plain session step. "Saved" only on a validated 200 for this customer, the name sent and the expected version. A conflict keeps the draft and offers "Review latest" (a read); a later save uses the reviewed version and a new key. An unknown outcome is held in memory only, with its form read-only, and checked again only by a click, with the same bytes, key, customer and token, within 23 hours. It is never re-sent automatically, and it is dropped on another token, an ended session, a user change or a sign-out. P6's Reconnect is unchanged. *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P2, commit `dc48862`: until then a held edit of the same user kept its token after a sign-out until a mounted page heard the end (DA-200); now a sign-out, heard from the edit hold store's module load whether or not a page is mounted, drops every user's held edit and recorded edit outcome whole, keeping only a per-user mark for one unnamed notice.)*

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *`name` optional, with the stored name read back into the answer.* The kernel would have to seal a response built from the store's answer: a kernel change for one route. Recorded as a deviation (DA-156).
- (b) *`If-Match` and `If-None-Match` processing.* The version travels in the body, which the fingerprint already binds; conditional processing would add a second, header-level contract to two routes.
- (c) *A version trigger on every change.* 023's create writes the contacts onto the row 020 has just inserted, so such a trigger would start every customer created with a contact at 2; and a table owner's raw UPDATE is the owner's (DA-129, amended).
- (d) *Calling 020's create for the authority decision.* It inserts. The steps are restated instead (ADR-35, amended).
- (e) *Changed field names in the audit metadata.* The writer's allow-list has no key for them, and widening the shared audit shape is a G-AUDIT decision (DA-158).
- (f) *An unsealed 409 for a stale version, as the role-level edit answers.* A same-key retry would be evaluated again. Sealing it fixes the retry's answer, as the tenant display-name command's update mode does.
- (g) *A rollback that resets versions or drops the audit column with its data.* An old editor's version could look current again, and audit evidence would be erased.

**Consequences.**
- **Closed.** Nothing. G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY stay OPEN; DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions; DA-153 and DA-155 stay open. Nothing is deployed.
- **Behaviour on change.** *Two editors on one version:* one 200, and one sealed 409 that writes only its completion. *A grant suspension, a session revocation or a level narrowing committed while the edit waits on 016's hold:* nothing written; *one attempted while the edit holds its locks:* it waits for the edit's COMMIT. *A tenant switch, a user change, a sign-out or an ended session:* the form, its draft and a held edit are dropped, never sent.
- **Supersedes or narrows.** ADR-34's "rename … stay open"; ADR-35's single derivation (a second copy at Edit); ADR-38 decision 1's "never idempotent" and decision 3's method rule; ADR-41 decisions 4 and 5 and the rollback order (amendments above).
- **Register.** DA-156 to DA-166 are new; DA-111, DA-113, DA-118, DA-120, DA-129, DA-136, DA-140, DA-150, DA-151, DA-152, DA-154 and DA-155 are amended (none closed); DA-153 is unchanged and open; DA-155 stays open.
- **Evidence.** Disposable PostgreSQL, local sockets, synthetic identities and synthetic data only (for example `Ada Lovelace`, `+1 555 0100`, `ada@example.test`):
  - `tests/db/customerEdit.integration.test.mjs` M7CE-01 to M7CE-11, run by a new CI step: apply over populated 001–023 rows with every tuple unchanged and every version 1; the login's privileges; patch semantics, with creation times never written; every refused argument; the Edit threshold and owner-edited levels; versions, no-ops and stale no-ops; two writers and revocations ordered by lock waits; the versioned profile read on one snapshot and its adapter; and the refused, exact and re-applied rollback;
  - `tests/db/customerEditHttp.integration.test.mjs` M7CH-01 to M7CH-09, run by a new CI step after every step that rolls 024 back: persistence read back through a second tenant entry; patch semantics and refusals; authority, the tenant boundary and the session witness; one change, one version, one audit record naming the customer, one completion, and the key's replay and target binding; audit and completion faults and a lost COMMIT answer; no-ops and replay before evaluation; two instances, two editors, one version; ordered revocations; and no value in any log line;
  - the unit suites of the route and its fingerprint target (`server/runtime/customerUpdate.test.ts`), the mutator (`server/persistence/postgresCustomerUpdate.test.ts`), the route table, the profile handler, the transactional store, the audit writer, the permissions, the startup preflight and the tenant entry;
  - the shop (commit `5253304`): `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-93 to SCD-146, `src/tenant/customersClient.test.ts` CDC-16 to CDC-19, and the browser scenarios SCB-11 (a persistent edit) and SCB-12 (a stale editor) of `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs`; the reachable role-state inventory (`src/context/reachableRoleStates.test.ts` C1) gains the Edit button's `customers:edit` call site, and C2 shows no decision changes with it.

  Exact-commit CI: run 36924348695 on `8e0c775` failed only the secret-scan reconciliation, because two synthetic UUID test fixtures (a patterned v4 Idempotency-Key and the RFC 4122 DNS-namespace UUID) matched the generic-api-key rule; the follow-up `3de6088` assembles both at run time with identical values and adds two owner-approved history-only manifest entries for the two historical lines, and its run 36990574252 is green. Run 36993931444 on `5253304` is green. Code-only: nothing is deployed, and no managed database was touched.

## ADR-43 — Searching server customers by name: an optional `q` on the list, matched case-insensitively by one session-bound definer routine, a cursor bound to the text, and no new index (DECIDED — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8)

**Context.** After M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 the server's customer list could only be paged in name order, so the shop's directory page could find a server customer only by paging (doc 05 §2; doc 09's open decision (i), search and sort). The owner brief for M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8 asks:
- a server-side search of customer names only, case-insensitive and literal, on the existing list route, with its unfiltered behaviour, response shape, limits and cursor unchanged;
- the tenant only from the trusted session, the list's permission, and the database deciding the authority again;
- no search text, name, contact or raw query in a log or an error, and none in the shop's address, history, storage or analytics;
- a shop search form with explicit submission, a draft separate from the applied search, "no matches" only after a successful empty answer, Reconnect as the only sign-in, and a reload after a confirmed create or edit;
- no new table, index, extension or dependency, and evidence of the query plan.

**Decision.**
1. **One route, one more key.** `GET /api/v1/customers` takes `q`, at most once, beside `limit` and `cursor`. It is form-decoded (`+` a space, then percent-decoding; a malformed escape, invalid UTF-8 or an encoded surrogate is 400), then trimmed. Blank is the unfiltered list, exactly as before. Otherwise it is refused (400) with a C0 or C1 control, DEL, U+2028, U+2029, a lone surrogate or a Bidi_Control character, or more than 200 code points. The whole raw query is bounded at 4,096 characters (it was 128). The policy, the response shape, the limits and the 401, 403, 429 and 503 answers are the list's. No refusal body, reason or log line carries the text.
2. **Name only, a literal substring, case-insensitive by uppercasing.** Both sides are uppercased by the ICU root locale's full mapping (`pg_catalog."und-x-icu"`), then compared by a LIKE under the "C" collation whose pattern escapes `\`, `%` and `_`, so nothing in the text is a wildcard. No normalization, accent folding or language rule is applied (doc 08 DA-169, DA-170).
3. **A cursor bound to the text.** A search page continues with tag 0x03, the last customer's id and the first 16 bytes of the SHA-256 of the normalized text: 33 bytes, 44 canonical base64url characters, carrying no text, name, tenant or authority. It is accepted only for the same normalized text, and each kind is refused on the other kind of request. Its position is the anchor's current name within the derived tenant, as for the list (DA-84).
4. **One session-bound definer routine (migration 025).** `tmpos_customer_search.m7a_customer_search`: plpgsql, `STABLE`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp`, `row_security = off`. It judges its arguments first (`invalid`), then restates 022's authority steps 1–6.5 statement for statement (one `denied` for any refusal), reads a continuation's anchor only in the derived tenant (`cursor_unknown`), and answers at most `p_limit` (1–101) matches in `(name collate "C", customer_id)` order from one statement with the tenant predicate inside it. It writes nothing and takes no row, advisory or write lock. The adapter calls it only for a search, with the authorizer's tenant as a precondition and `limit + 1`, and judges its rows with the list's own checks.
5. **Grants and readiness.** `tmpos_app` gets USAGE on the schema and EXECUTE on the routine, nothing else. The startup preflight requires exactly 001–025 and these grants; the tenant entry's reads readiness asks `probeCustomerSearch` last.
6. **The ICU collation is a precondition.** 025 refuses to apply where `und-x-icu` is absent, so a server without ICU never answers a different match.
7. **No new index.** The predicate is `upper(name collate "und-x-icu") collate "C" LIKE` the escaped `'%text%'` pattern, filtered to the derived tenant. The customer table keeps its two indexes, `customer_pkey` (`customer_id`) and 017's `customer_tenant_page_idx` (`tenant_id, name, customer_id`): the page index keeps a search to the tenant's range in page order and serves a continuation's keyset position, but no installed index evaluates the uppercase substring predicate itself. Measured locally (PostgreSQL 16.10, 25,000 synthetic customers, a tenant of 20,000): no match 8.459 ms and a rare match 8.279 ms, each a sequential scan of all 25,000 rows, and about 13 ms over the tenant's 20,000 index entries under a generic plan; a common match 0.121 ms and a continuation page 0.309 ms, both through the page index. A rare or absent text visits every customer in the tenant's range (or the whole table under a sequential plan), so its cost grows linearly, bounded by the read's 2.7 s statement limit and the per-client rate limit. No substring accelerator is installed: a trigram index would need the `pg_trgm` extension, which this work does not add. This is recorded, not a defect fixed here (DA-168).
8. **A rollback with no data guard.** 025 holds no data. Its down refuses only when 025 is not applied or its postcondition fails, and otherwise drops exactly its routine and schema, returning the 001–024 database. 025 rolls back first.
9. **The shop's search (commit `f326a4c`).** A form labelled "Search customers by name" on the server-backed list. Search or Enter applies the normalized draft and Clear the unfiltered list; nothing is sent per keystroke or for an invalid draft. The applied search belongs to the list state with its rows. "No customers match" appears only after a successful empty search page; every failure keeps the list's own state. Older answers and answers for another session, tenant or user are dropped; an ended session clears the rows, the cursor, the draft and the applied search, and a new session generation starts with an empty draft and the unfiltered list. Search actions follow the session client's plain rule: a plain check signs in only when no session is confirmed yet for this user or the cookie names another identity's session; after a confirmed end only the explicit Reconnect signs in *(wording corrected in M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10; it first read "never sign in")*. A confirmed create or edit reloads page one under the applied search; a confirmed edit first puts the confirmed name in the edited customer's shown row. The text never reaches the address, history, browser storage or analytics.
10. **A wording correction to ADR-42.** ADR-42 decision 4 is corrected in place: a current no-op records its sealed response as the key's idempotency completion. The behaviour is unchanged.

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *A separate search route.* It would need its own policy, refusals and cursor rules; the list's already fit, and a blank `q` leaves every existing request unchanged.
- (b) *`ILIKE`, `upper()` or `lower()` under the name's own collation.* The name column is `collate "C"`, under which they change ASCII letters only, so `é` would not match `É`. The ICU root collation is named explicitly instead.
- (c) *A substring index.* A trigram index, the kind that can serve an infix LIKE, needs the `pg_trgm` extension, which this stage does not add; a plain expression index on the uppercased name cannot serve a `'%text%'` pattern. Recorded for later (DA-168).
- (d) *The text inside the cursor, or no binding.* Carrying the text would put it in every continuation; no binding would let one search's cursor continue another. A digest binds without carrying (DA-171).
- (e) *Calling 022's read for the authority.* It answers one customer; the steps are restated, as 024 restated 020's (ADR-35, amended).
- (f) *Filtering in the shop.* The shop holds only the pages it has read, so a client-side filter would miss customers on unread pages.
- (g) *The search text in the page address.* It would reach history, bookmarks and referrers; the text stays in the page's memory.

**Consequences.**
- **Closed.** Nothing. G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY stay OPEN; DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. Nothing is deployed.
- **New pre-deployment requirement.** Access-log and edge redaction of `q`, and of the search cursor, on `GET /api/v1/customers` (DA-167, DA-171).
- **Behaviour on change.** *A rename or a create between two search pages:* a later page may repeat or skip a customer (DA-84). *A narrowing below View, a suspension or a revocation:* the next search is 403, decided by the authorizer and again by the routine on its snapshot. *An ICU change:* which names match may change (DA-169).
- **Supersedes or narrows.** ADR-32's 128-character query bound and DA-86's "search does not exist"; ADR-38's single copy of 022's derivation; ADR-42 decision 4's wording (amendments above).
- **Register.** DA-167 to DA-174 are new, DA-172 to DA-174 recording three M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 residuals once; DA-84, DA-86, DA-88, DA-102, DA-108 and DA-150 are amended (none closed); DA-152, DA-153, DA-155, DA-156, DA-158, DA-163, DA-165 and DA-166 stay open.
- **Evidence.** Disposable PostgreSQL, local sockets, synthetic identities and synthetic data only:
  - `tests/db/customerSearch.integration.test.mjs` M7CS-01 to M7CS-09, run by a new CI step after the customer edit's HTTP step: apply over populated 001–024 rows with every row byte-identical; the routine's attributes and privileges; the refused, exact and re-applied rollback; matching (whole, partial, ASCII and non-ASCII case, literal `%`, `_` and `\`, 200 code points, no match, a match beyond the first unfiltered page) and every refused argument; 130 matches among 180 customers walked at page sizes 1, 50 and 100 over duplicate names with no duplicate or omission; authority across two tenants with identical names and every standing as the list's routine decides it; the production adapter and probe; over HTTP, the shape, headers, cursor round trip, cross-text and cross-kind refusals, no echo in a body or log line and the 4,096 bound; a 403 below View; and no customer, audit, outbox or idempotency row written by a search;
  - `server/runtime/customerDirectory.test.ts` M7CS-U01 to M7CS-U08 (the parser, both cursor kinds, the handler, and over the enforced chain the 401, 403, 400, 429 and 503 with no echo), `server/persistence/postgresCustomerDirectory.test.ts` (dispatch, probe, answer checks), and the startup preflight and tenant entry suites;
  - the shop (commit `f326a4c`): `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-147 to SCD-165, `src/tenant/customersClient.test.ts` CDC-20 to CDC-22, and the browser scenarios SCB-13 (Search by button and Enter, Load more under a search, no match versus a lost request, Clear) and SCB-14 (a match opened, renamed out of the search, the active search read again from page one) of `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs`;
  - query plans: EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) of the routine's two statements over 25,000 synthetic customers (a tenant of 20,000): with literal parameters no match about 8.5 ms and a rare match about 8.3 ms, each by a sequential scan of every row (about 13 ms over the tenant's 20,000 index entries under a generic plan); a common match 0.12 ms and a continuation 0.31 ms on 017's `customer_tenant_page_idx`. Modest local evidence, not a benchmark.

  Exact-commit CI: run 37046119243 on `8ed4e56` and run 37048495769 on `f326a4c`, each green on attempt 1. This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation. Code-only: nothing is deployed, and no managed database was touched.

## ADR-44 — Draft customer references and the shared server customer picker (DECIDED — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P9)

**Context.** After M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8 the server's customers could be listed, searched, created and edited from the shop's Customer directory page, but every other screen still named customers only from the browser's own list. An invoice draft held a bare `customerId` string and found it in that list at save time, so a server id there would have made Save a silent no-op. Local ids (`c1` to `c5`, `c<timestamp>`, `c-<timestamp>`) and server ids (canonical UUIDs) are disjoint, but a bare string does not say which it is. Invoices are still created only in the browser's memory; durable invoices are M7b. The owner brief for M7A-CUSTOMERS-P9 asks for a server customer to be chosen in a new invoice draft without changing how existing invoices or local customers behave, and with no new route, migration, dependency or storage.

**Decision.**
1. **One tagged reference.** A draft names its customer by one frozen field (`src/tenant/customerReference.ts`): `none`, `local` (an id of the browser's list) or `server`. A draft therefore never holds an active local and an active server customer at once, and every reader states the source it accepts (`localCustomerIdOf` answers `''` for none and for a server reference, never a server id).
2. **Local ids and history keep their meaning.** Local ids are unchanged, and every historical invoice, repair and order reference stays as stored. Nothing is migrated, mapped, imported, merged or backfilled, and a name, phone or email never establishes identity (doc 08 DA-176).
3. **What a server reference holds.** The server-issued id (a branded type that only `serverCustomerReference` makes, from a list row whose id is canonical and whose name is non-empty), the name for display only, the UID of the shop user who chose it, and an opaque session context naming the checked session it was chosen under, compared by identity and holding no token or tenant. No contact field.
4. **React state only.** The draft lives in the invoice page's React state, as every local draft does: no browser storage, URL or log, and a reload loses it.
5. **One consumer.** Only the Create Invoice draft takes a server reference. Create and edit build an invoice only from a local reference, through the unchanged local lookup and snapshot; a server reference never reaches that lookup, `addInvoice`, `updateInvoice`, shipping or returns. Create stays unavailable for a server reference: Save is disabled, and the dialog explains that an invoice for a server customer cannot be created yet and to choose a customer from the browser's list instead. The edit dialog offers no picker.
6. **A shared picker over what exists.** `src/tenant/ServerCustomerPicker.tsx` reuses the existing list and search route (`GET /api/v1/customers`, `view_customers`), the shop's tenant session client and customers client, and P6's Reconnect rule: a reduced copy of the directory page's list path, not a refactor of it. It reads customers only; it never selects on its own; rows show the name and the end of the id. The entry point is offered on the plan's Customers feature (`canAccess('customers')`, the directory route's guard) and the local role state's Customers View hint, and a selection binds only while those and Invoices Create still hold. All three are checks in the browser: the server's 403 decides the customer read alone (`view_customers`; DA-140), and no server route creates or authorizes an invoice *(wording corrected in M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10: this sentence first read "the server's 403 decides" after all three)*.
7. **Session binding.** One generation per checked session token; a new generation also clears a running rate-limit wait. A window focus or a return to the tab runs one plain session check, in the open dialog (Select disabled until it answers; a return while a read is out is checked when that read settles) or, while the dialog is closed, in the draft's field. An ended or refused session, a 403 (unless its fresh session check finds a different token), or a different token drops a chosen customer with a notice; an ended check signs an open dialog out with Reconnect, and a different token starts a new generation. An undecided focus or tab-return check keeps the customer and the rows and starts no wait; after a 403 an undecided check leaves the refusal standing. A user switch, or lost Customers View or Customers feature, makes it none in the same render. Late answers from an earlier draft, a closed dialog or an earlier generation cannot bind. After a confirmed end only Reconnect signs in, and its token is the dialog's first, not a "connection changed" generation (DA-150). The module's UID and token pair is forgotten when the draft's field unmounts.
8. **Not authority.** A client-held reference authorizes nothing. M7b's durable invoice command must itself resolve the authenticated actor, the trusted tenant scope, the invoice permission and the customer's ownership and eligibility; a UUID or any client-held field satisfies none of these (DA-175).
9. **No server change.** No route, permission, migration, grant, dependency or browser storage is added.

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *Matching a server customer to a local one by name, phone or email.* Names and contacts are neither unique nor verified (DA-144, DA-153); a match would silently merge two people's records.
- (b) *Synthesising a legacy `Customer` object from a server row.* The invoice path would then treat a server id as local, and the browser's `Customer` record would need invented values for fields the server does not hand the list (email and phone are required there).
- (c) *Persisting the draft in browser storage.* No local draft is persisted, and the reference would outlive the session it was chosen under.
- (d) *Adding an invoice endpoint now.* Durable invoices, payments and their permissions are M7b's, under its own contracts.
- (e) *Extracting a shared list hook from the directory page.* Its list path is entangled with create and edit holds; a refactor would touch P5 to P8 behaviour.

**Consequences.**
- **Closed.** Nothing. G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY stay OPEN; DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. Nothing is deployed.
- **Behaviour.** A new invoice draft can hold a server customer but cannot be created with it (DA-175). Existing invoices, local customers and every other screen behave as before; repair drafts do not use the picker yet (DA-177; since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10 the new repair ticket draft does, ADR-45).
- **Session.** The picker is the session token's third in-memory holder (doc 03). A Reconnect in the picker signs in and ends the session presented, which another open tab sees as a changed connection, as any Reconnect does (DA-137).
- **Register.** DA-175 to DA-179 are new; DA-140, DA-144 and DA-150 are amended (none closed).
- **Evidence.** jsdom suites over the real session and customers clients and a reduced fake server at the fetch boundary:
  - `src/tenant/ServerCustomerPicker.test.tsx` SCP-01 to SCP-47: the reference and the context; page one, Load more and search beyond page one; identical names; every list state, empty and failure kept distinct; rate limits on a fake clock (exact `Retry-After`, 60 s without one); signed-out, ended and Reconnect (a first token, no "changed" notice); a token change mid-read; 403 with one fresh check; stale, closed and unmounted reads; the field's change, remove, cancel, focus checks and user switch; the open dialog's focus check (changed, undecided, ended, skipped when signed out, late after unmount); a new generation clearing a wait; the module pair forgotten on unmount; focus kept in the dialog; request headers and no storage, cookie or console;
  - `src/components/Invoices.serverCustomer.test.tsx` INV-01 to INV-15: the real Invoices page with a local create unchanged, a server reference never reaching `addInvoice`, Save disabled with the limit text (and described by it), no invoice from selecting, changing, removing or cancelling, user switch, reopened drafts, lost permissions, a plan without the Customers feature and the edit dialog unchanged;
  - `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SCB-15: the real shop in a browser (see doc 09);
  - `src/context/reachableRoleStates.test.ts` C1 gains `customers:view`; frontend lane 26 files / 679 cases; shop browser gate 29.

  Exact-commit CI: run 37075869185 on `a5f1416`, green on attempt 1, and run 37108347751 on `17a659b`, which failed and stays red (the later green runs do not change it): every step was green but the dependency audit, which refused two High advisories against `@fastify/busboy`, GHSA-xjh9-v7x6-24jw (CVE-2026-19484) and GHSA-x8mw-p69m-v3mx (CVE-2026-19481). Both were disclosed in August: the GitHub advisories' `nvd_published_at` field dates both CVEs' NVD records 2026-08-13. They entered GitHub's advisory database on 2026-10-02 (its `published_at` field: 23:16:17Z and 23:16:36Z), while run 37075869185 was in progress; that run's dependency audit step started at 23:26:44Z (the step's `startedAt`) and passed. *(Corrected in M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10: this record first said "published on 2026-10-02 at 23:16 UTC, after the first run", naming no source field.)* Commit `b19dfc4` moves that package to 3.2.2 within firebase-admin's declared range (lockfile only; the security manifest and the audit checker unchanged), and run 37109960449 on it is green on attempt 1. P8's documentation commit `d152702` ran green as run 37051807118 on attempt 1. This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation. Code-only: nothing is deployed, and no managed database was touched.

## ADR-45 — The repair draft's server customer and the picker as a keyboard modal (DECIDED — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10)

**Context.** ADR-44 let a new invoice draft name a server customer through a shared picker. The new repair ticket form still held a bare `customerId` string (a local id, or empty for a typed walk-in) and could not name one (DA-177). The picker was `role="dialog"` with `aria-modal="true"` but did not contain focus: Tab could leave it for the page behind, the page behind stayed reachable, and its overlay's `z-[60]` was below the repair form's `z-[110]` (DA-178). No test covered a 403 whose fresh session check was undecided (DA-179). P9's records also put an invoice permission that no server checks under "the server's 403 decides", earlier records said plain session steps "never sign in", and P9's CI record dated the `@fastify/busboy` advisories without naming a source field. The owner brief for M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10 asks for the repair-draft integration under the same contract, the picker's keyboard contract and those corrections, with no new route, migration, dependency or storage.

**Decision.**
1. **The same reference in the repair draft.** The new ticket form's `customerId` becomes `customer: DraftCustomerReference` (`src/components/RepairTickets.tsx`): `none` (nothing, or a typed walk-in name), `local` (a pick from the browser's list, `localCustomerReference`) or `server` (the shared field). `customerName`, `customerPhone` and `customerEmail` remain the local snapshot and are empty for a server customer. A walk-in id (`walk-in-<timestamp>`) is still minted at save only and is never a draft reference; a ticket's `customerId` is `localCustomerIdOf(customer)` or that walk-in id, never a server id. Typing in the customer box or picking a local customer replaces a server reference; Remove clears the binding only. Editing a ticket and the ticket detail are unchanged.
2. **One rule for both drafts.** `usableDraftCustomer` (`src/tenant/customerReference.ts`, moved from the invoice page with the same expression) makes a server reference none unless the signed-in UID chose it and the Customers View hint holds; each draft uses it in the render and drops the reference in an effect.
3. **The field and its gates.** `ServerCustomerField` sits below the customer box, keyed by the user and the draft (`${uid}:${draftId}`). It is offered on `canAccess('customers') && checkPermission('customers', 'view')`; a reported choice binds only for the open draft (`liveDraft`) and only while `checkPermission('repairs', 'create')` and both customer checks still hold. Every close (Cancel, the backdrop, a created ticket) ends the draft and drops a server reference.
4. **The save/create boundary.** With a server customer Create Ticket is disabled and described (`aria-describedby`) by `SERVER_REPAIR_LIMIT_TEXT`: "This ticket uses a customer saved on the server. Creating a repair ticket for a server customer isn't available yet, so this ticket can't be created. To create it here, type the customer's name or choose a customer from this browser's list instead." `handleCreateTicket` returns first on a server reference: no ticket, no reset, no close. A server selection fills no contact, device or other field and reaches no storage, URL or log. Local and walk-in tickets are created exactly as before.
5. **Scope and authorization.** The plan's Customers feature, Customers View and Repairs Create (like Invoices Create in ADR-44) are checks in the browser: presentation and enforcement in that tab only. The server's 403 decides customer reads alone (`GET /api/v1/customers`, `view_customers`); no server route creates, stores or authorizes a repair ticket or an invoice, and a client-held reference authorizes nothing. Durable repairs are M7d's: their command must itself resolve the authenticated actor, the trusted tenant scope, the repair permission and the customer's ownership and eligibility (DA-180). *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P1, [ADR-47](#adr-47--store-owned-invoice-drafts-under-real-store-grant-authority-decided--m7b-invoices-p1): invoice drafts' save, list and reopen only. Server routes now create, store and authorize store-owned invoice drafts; no server route creates or authorizes a repair ticket or any other invoice, and the browser checks above are unchanged.)*
6. **The picker as a keyboard modal** (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerPicker.tsx`). The overlay is `z-[190]`, above every host dialog it opens from (the invoice dialog's `z-50`, the repair form's `z-[110]`) and below the shop's `z-[200]` layers (toasts, and the POS supervisor-authorization dialog, which never hosts the picker). While the dialog is open every other child that `<body>` had when it opened gets the `inert` attribute, in a layout effect whose cleanup removes exactly what it set (a node that was already inert stays so). Tab and Shift+Tab wrap around the dialog's enabled buttons and inputs, and from anything else land on the first or the last. Escape is default-prevented and stopped, so it closes the picker only and a host dialog stays open. A press on the backdrop is default-prevented. A press on the dialog's own text that is no control focuses the dialog itself (`tabIndex={-1}`: no Tab stop and no ring of its own), so Escape and Tab still reach it. Focus opens on the search box and returns on close to "Select a server customer", or to Change when a customer is held; when a check while the dialog is closed drops the held customer with focus on Change or Remove, the field moves focus to "Select a server customer", and focus elsewhere stays put. Every control has a `focus-visible` outline.
7. **Session wording, not behaviour.** The session client's rule is unchanged: a plain check signs in only when no session is confirmed yet for this user or the cookie names another identity's session; after a confirmed end only the explicit Reconnect signs in. Comments in the picker and the directory page, and records in docs 03, 05, 08, 09 and 10 that said a plain step "never signs in", now state it.
8. **No server change.** No route, permission, migration, grant, dependency or browser storage is added; the tenant entry still serves twelve routes, and migrations stay 001–025.

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *A repair-specific picker or list path.* The shared field already carries the session, generation, denial and keyboard rules; a copy would drift.
- (b) *Matching a typed walk-in name to a server customer.* Names are neither unique nor verified (DA-144, DA-153); a typed name stays a walk-in.
- (c) *Storing the server id on a browser ticket.* The browser's ticket would name an id its customer list cannot resolve, with no server record behind it (DA-176); durable repairs are M7d's.
- (d) *Making the invoice and repair dialogs keyboard modals in this stage.* They are among the shop's older modals, with no `role="dialog"`, Escape handling or focus containment of their own; that is accessibility work across those modals (G-A11Y, DA-181).

**Consequences.**
- **Closed.** DA-177 for the delivered repair-draft integration only; DA-178 at code and browser level for the picker dialog. No gate closes: G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY stay OPEN; DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. Nothing is deployed.
- **Behaviour.** A new repair ticket can hold a server customer but cannot be created with it (DA-180), as an invoice draft cannot (DA-175). Local and walk-in tickets, editing, the ticket detail and every stored ticket behave as before; the POS, returns, shipping and the other screens that name customers are unchanged. The picker is a keyboard modal in both drafts; the dialogs around it are not (DA-181).
- **Session.** No new holder: the repair draft uses the same field and module, still the session token's third in-memory holder (doc 03).
- **Register.** DA-180, DA-181 and DA-182 are new; DA-177 is closed for the repair draft and DA-178 at code and browser level; DA-179 is narrowed; DA-140 and DA-150 are amended. DA-175, DA-176, DA-155 (contact retention) and DA-167 and DA-168 (the search's log redaction and cost) stay open.
- **Corrections.** ADR-44 Decision 6, doc 05, doc 09 and DA-140 now say that Invoices Create is a browser check and that the 403 decides customer reads only. ADR-43, docs 03, 05, 08 and 09 and DA-150 no longer say plain steps "never sign in". ADR-44's CI record names the advisories' source fields and keeps run 37108347751 red; commit `b19dfc4`'s message keeps the earlier wording, since history is not rewritten.
- **Evidence.** jsdom suites over the real session and customers clients and a reduced fake server at the fetch boundary, and the shop in a real browser:
  - `src/tenant/ServerCustomerPicker.test.tsx` SCP-48 to SCP-57: a 403 whose one fresh check is unavailable (SCP-48) or rate-limited (SCP-49), where the refusal stands, the draft drops its customer with the denied notice, the session is never asked to reconnect and late answers restore nothing; after a confirmed end no focus, tab return or reopen signs in, only Reconnect (SCP-50); Tab and Shift+Tab wrap and skip disabled controls (SCP-51, SCP-52); inert set and exactly restored (SCP-53); Escape inside a host dialog closes only the picker, and focus returns (SCP-54); a backdrop press moves no focus (SCP-55); a press on the dialog's text focuses the dialog, from which Escape and Tab still work (SCP-56); a drop while the dialog is closed moves focus from Change or Remove to "Select a server customer" and leaves focus elsewhere alone (SCP-57, three cases);
  - `src/components/RepairTickets.serverCustomer.test.tsx` REP-01 to REP-18 (20 cases): the real RepairTickets page, with local, walk-in and technician tickets as before, a server reference never reaching a ticket, Create disabled and described with no ticket by any path (REP-05 pinning that a server reference leaves the local name snapshot empty), replacement by typing or a local pick, every close, late reports and reads from a closed draft, a user switch, lost permissions, and no storage, URL or log;
  - `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SCB-16 (the repair draft) and SCB-17 and SCB-18 (the keyboard journey in the invoice and the repair draft; see doc 09);
  - `src/context/reachableRoleStates.test.ts` C1 unchanged: the repair draft's `checkPermission('customers', 'view')` adds no pair to that inventory, which already holds `customers:view` from the invoice page. Frontend lane 27 files / 711 cases; shop browser gate 32; main runner 162 suites (unchanged: it counts `.ts` and `.mjs` suites only; `src/components/RepairTickets.serverCustomer.test.tsx` is a new required frontend suite and shop sentinel).

  Commit `c46d3eb` is the picker's keyboard contract (Decision 6) and Decision 7's comment corrections in the picker and the directory page, with SCP-48 to SCP-57 and SCB-17 (frontend lane 26 files / 691 cases); commit `5eeda24` is the repair draft and the move of `usableDraftCustomer` (Decisions 1 to 5) with REP-01 to REP-18, SCB-16 and SCB-18 (27 files / 711 cases). Exact-commit CI: run 37152646550 on `c46d3eb`, which failed on attempt 1 and passed all 66 steps on attempt 2 (attempt 1's only red step was the main runner, 161 of 162 suites: `server/runtime/customerCreate.test.ts`'s contact-free refusal check met a random `requestId` containing `555`, DA-182; commit A changed no server file, and the passing attempt does not repair the check) and run 37154579169 on `5eeda24`, which passed all 66 steps on attempt 1. P9's documentation commit `706c319` ran green as run 37111259805 on attempt 1. This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation. Code-only: nothing is deployed, and no managed database was touched.

## ADR-46 — Server-backed Invoices role levels are owner-managed configuration (DECIDED — M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1)

**Context.** After M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10 the server stored an owner's level for the built-in roles in one domain only, Customers (ADR-35 to ADR-37). Invoices are still created and kept in the browser; durable invoices are M7b, and M7b's first operation cannot be designed until the owner settles several questions (below). The catalog already defines Invoices as a core tenant domain with defaults (`manager` Full, `technician` View, `sales_staff` Create; `store_owner` Full), and the server's resolver already applies a stored level for any tenant domain. What limited storage to Customers was 020's and 021's domain CHECKs, 021's routines (hard-coded to `customers`) and the runtime's domain list. M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1 makes the built-in roles' Invoices level owner-managed on the server ahead of M7b, without claiming any invoice operation is server-enforced. The stage also repairs a flaky server test (doc 08 DA-182).

**Decision.**
1. **Separate invoices-only routines.** Migration 026 adds schema `tmpos_role_invoice` with `m7a_invoice_role_level_states(uuid)` (STABLE) and `m7a_invoice_role_level_set(text, text, text, uuid, bigint)` (VOLATILE): 021's states read and conditional edit, the same derivation, owner rule, preconditions and lock order, with the domain fixed to `invoices` inside each, so an Invoices edit can never write a Customers row. Both are `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp` and `row_security = off`; EXECUTE and schema USAGE go to `tmpos_app` only. One schema per migration follows 020's and 021's precedent: their postconditions pin their schemas' inventories.
2. **Widened CHECKs, nothing else in the existing tables.** 020's `tenant_role_level_domain_chk` and 021's `role_level_version_domain_chk` become `domain in ('customers', 'invoices')` under the same names. 021's trigger already versions per (tenant, role, domain).
3. **The Customers path is unchanged.** The runtime keeps calling 021's `m7a_role_level_set_v2` and `m7a_role_level_states` for Customers; 020's and 021's objects and grants are untouched, and `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` answers byte for byte as before.
4. **One PUT, a sibling read.** `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` accepts `domain: "invoices"` in its unchanged six-field body; the mutator runs a fixed statement per domain, and an unknown domain is unavailable before any statement. `GET /api/v1/roles/levels/invoices` reads the Invoices levels back under the same requirement and validation (doc 05 §2 holds both contracts).
5. **No new permission key.** The configuration authority is `edit_role_levels` under the owner rule, as for Customers: never an Invoices level and never a Customers level. No role default, level ordering or catalog entry changes, and no route decides on the stored Invoices level. *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P1, [ADR-47](#adr-47--store-owned-invoice-drafts-under-real-store-grant-authority-decided--m7b-invoices-p1): invoice drafts' save, list and reopen only. Those three routes now decide on the stored level, through two new store route permissions; no other route does, and no role default or level ordering changes.)* *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P2, [ADR-48](#adr-48--editing-a-saved-store-invoice-draft-at-invoices-edit-versioned-and-idempotent-with-sealed-conflicts-and-no-ops-decided--m7b-invoices-p2): the draft edit and its edit eligibility also decide on the stored level, through a third store route permission, `edit_invoice_drafts`; the creation eligibility decides on the save's. No other route does, and no role default or level ordering changes.)*
6. **Runtime and migration in lockstep.** The startup preflight requires exactly 001–026 and 026's three grants, so an earlier runtime refuses a 026 database and this runtime refuses a 025 one.
7. **Configuration, labelled as such.** The shop's second panel, "Server-saved Invoices level", says that invoices and their actions are not enforced by the server, and the grid's Invoices row points built-in roles to it. The shop's invoice screens still decide on the browser's local role state (doc 08 DA-183). *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P1, [ADR-47](#adr-47--store-owned-invoice-drafts-under-real-store-grant-authority-decided--m7b-invoices-p1): invoice drafts' save, list and reopen only. The panel now says the server uses the level to decide who may save, list and open server drafts at a store, and that other invoices are not checked by the server.)* *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P2, [ADR-48](#adr-48--editing-a-saved-store-invoice-draft-at-invoices-edit-versioned-and-idempotent-with-sealed-conflicts-and-no-ops-decided--m7b-invoices-p2), commit `4f3c700`: the panel now says the server uses the level to decide who may save, list, open and edit server drafts at a store.)*
8. **No automatic PUT re-send (correction, M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1-R1).** P1's report said a lost reply was re-sent once, automatically; the shared panel did that for both domains, against the rule that only an explicit owner action sends a mutation. The re-send is removed: one Save press sends at most one PUT. A PUT with no answer is held with its key and exact change, says "Not confirmed yet — press Save again to check", and is re-sent only by the owner's next Save, under the same key and with byte-identical bytes. No timer, read, focus or visibility change, session renewal or Reconnect sends it. The server's idempotency, lease, version and audit contracts are unchanged.

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *Widening 021's routines to take a domain.* 021's file is frozen with its checksum, and a domain parameter would let one call path write either domain; fixed per-domain routines keep an Invoices edit away from Customers rows by construction.
- (b) *A new permission key for invoice configuration.* The owner rule already names who may change role levels; a second key would need its own grant semantics with no gain.
- (c) *Enforcing invoice operations now.* No invoice route, table or lifecycle exists, and the owner decisions below are open; a stored level is the one piece that needs none of them.
- (d) *Adopting the invoice-draft shape proposed after M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10.* Not adopted as policy: it rests on the open decisions below.

**Consequences.**
- **Closed.** DA-182 (test code only, `ece44c6`). No gate closes: G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY stay OPEN; DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. Nothing is deployed, and 026 has been applied to no managed database.
- **Behaviour.** The owner can read and set the Invoices level of `manager`, `technician` and `sales_staff` on the server; nothing a team member can do with invoices changes (DA-183). The materialized Invoices permission is proven in unit tests, not over HTTP (DA-184). **Amended (M7B-INVOICES-P1, [ADR-47](#adr-47--store-owned-invoice-drafts-under-real-store-grant-authority-decided--m7b-invoices-p1)).** The stored level now decides the store-owned drafts' save, list and reopen, and no other invoice operation (DA-183 narrowed); an owner's edit decides the save and the list over HTTP in both directions (DA-184 closed for those two). Of the open decisions below, the owner has settled ownership (a real store within a tenant), the first operations (save, list and reopen a draft), the draft lifecycle for this slice (status `draft` only) and the currency (USD in integer cents); the rest stay open. *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P2, [ADR-48](#adr-48--editing-a-saved-store-invoice-draft-at-invoices-edit-versioned-and-idempotent-with-sealed-conflicts-and-no-ops-decided--m7b-invoices-p2): it now also decides the draft edit (DA-183 amended).)*
- **Deploy and rollback.** 026 rolls back first. Its down refuses while any `invoices` row exists in either table (configuration or version history), so after any Invoices edit a rollback needs the table owner to delete those rows deliberately (DA-122, amended).
- **Register.** DA-182 closed; DA-183, DA-184 and DA-185 new; DA-85, DA-119, DA-121, DA-122, DA-124 and DA-125 amended. ADR-35, ADR-36 and ADR-37 amended.
- **Still open (owner decisions before M7b's first invoice operation).**
  - whether invoices are owned by the tenant or by a store;
  - the draft lifecycle and its route rules;
  - currency and amounts;
  - numbering, tax, discounts and issuing;
  - draft retention;
  - the mapping of legacy browser customers to server customers (DA-176);
  - which M7b operation comes first, and in what sequence.
- **Evidence.** Disposable PostgreSQL, local sockets and a real browser only:
  - `tests/db/invoiceRoleLevelRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M5IRL-01 to M5IRL-09: apply and postcondition, inventory and effective privileges, defaults, refusals, preconditions, reset-to-default ABA, isolation from Customers and between tenants, the upgrade over a 025 database holding Customers edits, and the refusing and exact rollback;
  - `tests/db/invoiceRoleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M5IRLH-01 to M5IRLH-09 over the tenant entry: defaults, a durable edit read back by a second entry, isolation, the edit's and the read's authority, the precondition, two entries racing, atomicity on an audit fault, and idempotency including a lost COMMIT answer;
  - `tests/db/roleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M7RLH-C1 and `tests/db/tenantEntry.integration.test.mjs` M7TE-01 (thirteen routes);
  - `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SRL-13 and SRL-14;
  - the unit suites of the edit, the directory, the adapter, the preflight, the canonical permissions, the entry and the composition, and of the shop (`src/tenant/ServerInvoiceLevels.test.tsx` SIL-01 onward; `src/tenant/roleLevelsClient.test.ts` RLC-10 to RLC-12). The frontend ratchet requires at least 28 files / 729 cases; shop browser gate 34; main runner 162 suites.

  Commit `ece44c6` is the DA-182 test repair, commit `fc42452` the server, migration, database suites and CI steps, and commit `e0b9ddf` the shop and its browser cases. Exact-commit CI: run 37189788170 on `ece44c6`, green on attempt 1; run 37201875568 on `fc42452`, green on attempt 1; run 37203237043 on `e0b9ddf`, green on attempt 1. This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation. Code-only: nothing is deployed, and no managed database was touched.


## ADR-47 — Store-owned invoice drafts under real store-grant authority (DECIDED — M7B-INVOICES-P1)

**Context.** ADR-46 left M7b's first invoice operation waiting on owner decisions. For this stage the owner decided that an invoice draft is owned by a real store within a tenant, that the first operations are save, list and reopen (read-only), that the only status is `draft`, and that amounts are USD in integer cents; customers stay tenant-owned. Until now no route was store-scoped: the authorizer counted only tenant-scope grants for tenant routes (ADR-33), every store permission was undecidable, and the tenant session record names no tenant or store. No accepted document establishes tenant-to-store inheritance (doc 04 §2.1, for the store-scoped payment-gateway permissions, denies a tenant-only or other-store membership), and the earlier proposed invoice-draft shape was not policy (ADR-46 (d)). The stage needed store authority that a path, a body, a browser label or a store row's existence cannot establish.

**Decision.**
1. **Store authority is an explicit store grant at exactly the requested store.** A store route is authorized only through the actor's own active `user_membership` row with `scope_type = 'store'` at the store the path names, whose tenant is the store row's. Exactly one: none, two roles at one store (ambiguous), a grant at another store, a tenant-scope grant, and a missing, foreign or non-canonical store are one 403. The path's store is a requested target that selects among the actor's grants; a store row's existence proves nothing. No tenant-to-store inheritance: a tenant-only owner reaches no store. The owner's fixed Full applies only at stores where the owner holds a store grant.
2. **Two store route permissions, decidable at store scope only.** `view_invoice_drafts` (Invoices at View or above) and `create_invoice_drafts` (Invoices at Create or above and, on the same role, Customers at View or above), on the store grant's role at its tenant's owner-edited levels (026's and 020's rows; the catalog defaults otherwise; `store_owner` Full; built-in roles only). At tenant scope they name nothing, and no other permission, default or ordering changes. The authorizer and migration 028's routines decide all three operations; the save, the one write, is also re-decided inside its command transaction by the revalidator, which re-selects the grant at the trusted store (list and reopen are reads and run in no command transaction). *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P1-R2, commit `6b5cef8`: a read-only `GET /api/v1/stores/{storeId}/invoice-drafts/eligibility` declares the save's own requirement object (`INVOICE_DRAFT_CREATE_REQUIREMENT`), so the one tenant authorizer and evaluator answer "may this actor save here now" exactly as they decide the save, with no second evaluator: `200 { "eligibility": "allowed" }` or one 403, no business row written (doc 05 §2). The answer is a snapshot; the save still decides again under its locks. No permission, default, grant or inheritance changes.)* *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P2, [ADR-48](#adr-48--editing-a-saved-store-invoice-draft-at-invoices-edit-versioned-and-idempotent-with-sealed-conflicts-and-no-ops-decided--m7b-invoices-p2): a third, `edit_invoice_drafts` (Invoices at Edit or above, no Customers level), authorizes the draft edit and its edit eligibility; the edit is decided again in its transaction and, on the change path, by migration 029's routine on the locked role row.)*
3. **Discovery is a closed session-bound route.** `GET /api/v1/stores` runs no authorizer: migration 027's definer derives, from the session key alone, every store where the actor holds exactly one usable grant (at most 200), with its tenant, servable name and read-only flag. It is the only business route of that shape, pinned at registration.
4. **One atomic save.** `POST /api/v1/stores/{storeId}/invoice-drafts` is the typed command `store.invoice_draft.create` carrying values only. One transaction fences the lease, revalidates the store scope, runs 028's create (customer of the store's tenant checked before any write; header and lines; subtotal re-verified), writes one store-scope audit record naming the draft, appends `invoice_draft.created` v1 `{ storeId, customerId, lineCount }` and seals the 201 completion before COMMIT. Input is closed and bounded (1–50 lines; descriptions by the customer name rule at 256 scalars; whole quantities; non-negative cents; BigInt totals within 2^53−1; 64 KiB).
5. **A typed sealed refusal for the customer.** A missing customer and another tenant's are one sealed 422 `customer_not_found`, decided after authority and before any write, committed as the key's completion and replayed; another `expectedTenant` is the unsealed 409 `write_conflict`. No other command's refusal, conflict or lease behaviour changes.
6. **Context scope is declared and checked.** Every command contract and aggregate mutator declares a context scope; composition refuses a route, contract and mutator that disagree, with no default (`command_scope_mismatch`), at the tenant root (`tenant` or `store`) and at the administrative root (`platform`, DA-195).
7. **Reads re-derive and never trust a timestamp.** List and reopen re-derive authority from the session key with the trusted tenant and store as preconditions; the list cursor names only the anchor draft, whose time is re-read inside the scope, and an unknown, foreign or malformed cursor is one 400; on reopen a missing and a foreign draft are one 404.
8. **Unknown outcomes stay unknown.** A lost COMMIT answer is a 503 with the lease kept; only an explicit retry with the same key and bytes resolves it, under current authority. The shop re-sends only on "Check again", never on its own, and detaches a held save on a user change (once the new user's Invoices page renders) or a session-token change; a sign-out unmounts the page and scrubs nothing (DA-198). *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P1 closing round, commit `e79251d`: a sign-out now detaches it too, through the session client's `onSessionEnd`, which runs at the start of every logout, before anything is sent or awaited and whether or not the page is mounted, and takes the token from every user's attempt; DA-198 is closed for the drafts and the picker, and the Customer directory page's residual is DA-200.)* *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P1-R2, commit `68bc927`: the Customer directory's create hold store is bound to the same signal from its module's load; a sign-out takes every held create's token and whole body, and an attempt so detached keeps its key and name only to settle a late answer by key, never re-sent, read back or moved to a new session (DA-200 closed for the create hold store).)* *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P2, commit `dc48862`: the Customer directory's edit hold store now hears the same session end from its module's load; held edits and recorded edit outcomes lose their token, body, contacts and name, and only a per-user mark for one unnamed notice stays (DA-200 closed in full). No erasure is claimed: bytes already sent and copies the JavaScript engine keeps are not erased.)*
9. **Separate in the shop.** Server drafts have their own section, types and client; they never enter the browser's invoices, and no amount due, number, issued status or payment action is shown. *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P1-R2, commit `33972c0`: the section offers "New server draft" only on the eligibility route's exact `allowed` for the current user, generation, store and request; the local role state no longer decides it (DA-201).)* *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P2, [ADR-48](#adr-48--editing-a-saved-store-invoice-draft-at-invoices-edit-versioned-and-idempotent-with-sealed-conflicts-and-no-ops-decided--m7b-invoices-p2), commit `4f3c700`: the reopen dialog offers Edit only on the edit eligibility's exact `allowed` for the user, generation, store, draft and request.)*

**Alternatives not taken.**
- (a) *Tenant-to-store inheritance (a tenant grant reaching every store).* No accepted document establishes it, and the stage brief forbids inventing it; recorded as an owner decision (DA-188).
- (b) *Relabelling a tenant scope as a store, or reading the store from the body or the session.* Each would make a client value or a session-store key authority.
- (c) *Store-scope customer reads (DA-87 Option B).* Not decided; the picker keeps the existing tenant customer reads, and store-only actors cannot pick (DA-189).
- (d) *A plain foreign key on `customer_id`.* It would not prove the customer's tenant; 028 adds the composite UNIQUE constraints instead.
- (e) *Answering a missing customer with a 503 or a 404.* A sealed typed 422 ends the attempt honestly and replays.

**Consequences.**
- **Closed.** Nothing at gate level: G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY stay OPEN; DA-118 and DA-119 remain pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. DA-184 is closed for the draft save and list. *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P1 closing round, commit `fa3dfd5`: DA-184 is now closed in full, the reopen included: IDH-03 drives it through the owner's own `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` over HTTP in both directions, and IDR-05 drives the get routine through the owner's level edits.)* Nothing is deployed, and 027 and 028 have been applied to no managed database.
- **Behaviour.** A team member with a store grant can save, list and reopen USD drafts at that store as the owner-edited levels allow; every other invoice stays in the browser and decides on the local role state (DA-183 narrowed). An owner's level edit is shown deciding the save and the list; the reopen's Invoices View check is decided in code (028's `m7b_invoice_draft_get`; the authorizer's unit M7B-AZ-01) but no test drives it through an owner edit (DA-184). *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P1 closing round, commit `fa3dfd5`: tests now drive it, over HTTP (`tests/db/invoiceDraftsHttp.integration.test.mjs` IDH-03: a `technician` at View reopens, at None gets the list's own 403, back at View reopens the same draft) and on the get routine (`tests/db/invoiceDraftRoutine.integration.test.mjs` IDR-05).)* A read-only account, tenant or store reads only. Drafts cannot be edited or deleted (DA-186). *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P2, [ADR-48](#adr-48--editing-a-saved-store-invoice-draft-at-invoices-edit-versioned-and-idempotent-with-sealed-conflicts-and-no-ops-decided--m7b-invoices-p2): drafts can be edited at Invoices Edit or above; they still cannot be discarded or deleted (DA-186).)*
- **Deploy and rollback.** The runtime and 027–028 move together (preflight: exactly 001–028). 028's up and down lock five (down: seven) tables `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` before any check, a maintenance window. 028 rolls back first and refuses while any draft, line or draft-naming audit record exists; 027's down refuses while 028 is applied. A store holding drafts cannot move to another tenant, and a tenant, store or customer holding drafts cannot be deleted (DA-186). *(Amended M7B-INVOICES-P2, [ADR-48](#adr-48--editing-a-saved-store-invoice-draft-at-invoices-edit-versioned-and-idempotent-with-sealed-conflicts-and-no-ops-decided--m7b-invoices-p2): the runtime and 029 now move together (preflight: exactly 001–029); 029 rolls back first and refuses while any draft's version is above 1, then 028 as stated.)*
- **Register.** DA-183 and DA-175 narrowed; DA-184 closed for the save and list; DA-60 amended; DA-186 to DA-199 new (DA-194 resolved in code at `15110e4`).
